His baptism in Nybøl in 1903 no. 12 identifies his parents as farmer Peter Petersen and Anna Cathrine Marie Petersen. A family grave photograph gives the same dates and names Påkjær. The 1921 Nybøl census is useful for the household and farm context but does not prove Lorenz's paternity.","provenance":"Tidligere Lorenz-dossier, fuldt indarbejdet 16.11.2026","imported_on":"2026-11-16"},{"title":"Keep three roles separate","context":"2. Lorenz's paternity — central conflict","text":"| Role | Person | Current evidence |\n|---|---|---|\n| Registered/legal father | Wilhelm Krogh | Civil-register marginal note, paternity declaration and court decision reported; underlying file not yet inspected |\n| Family-claimed biological father | Sophus Petersen | Family account and chart; disputed, not independently established |\n| Social/stepfather | Peter Frederiksen | Church-book entry and family account |\n\nDo not rewrite the biological father field as settled. The canonical data no longer sets `father: sofus` as a settled parent link. Both the official register's biological-father designation for Wilhelm and the family's biological-father claim for Sophus are retained as separate, disputed relationship assertions. The step/social father Peter remains a separate link. This prevents the visual tree and downstream exports from presenting either disputed biological claim as established.","provenance":"Tidligere Lorenz-dossier, fuldt indarbejdet 16.11.2026","imported_on":"2026-11-16"},{"title":"Archive route","context":"2. Lorenz's paternity — central conflict","text":"Rigsarkivet's current guidance says paternity cases from 1919–1970 generally began with police; disputed paternity was decided by civil court; maintenance/alimentation files are held by the county archive. The recommended first route is the county archive's alimentation journal/register and case file, followed by the civil court's paternity case list and judgment book, and then the police journal if needed. Search using Else Gedde Frederiksen/Jensen, Lorenz (born 12 January 1932), Wilhelm Krogh of Snogbæk, the 5 April declaration, 10 June decision and 11 July marginal-note dates.\n\n**New archival finding — exact county archive and a date-boundary trap (researched 16 November 2026):**\n- The official Rigsarkivet archive overview identifies both the **Sønderborg Amt archive (1920–1932)** and the **Aabenraa-Sønderborg Amt archive (1920–1971)**. It says the Sønderjylland county archives are journal archives with registers and case files, and that the Aabenraa/Aabenraa-Sønderborg holdings include *alimentationssager*. The 1920–1935 journal/register/case-file finding aid is unprinted and must be consulted in the Aabenraa reading room; the overview specifically lists an internal handover register for the Sønderborg Amt archive.\n- The same overview states that Aabenraa and Sønderborg counties were merged with effect from **1 April 1932**. The Egernsund birth occurred on 12 January 1932, but the paternity declaration and judgment cited in the marginal note are dated **5 April and 10 June 1932**, respectively. Therefore do not assume all three documents are filed under the same county archive creator: inspect both **Sønderborg Amt** (for the January birth / any opening record) and **Aabenraa-Sønderborg Amt** (for the April–June 1932 declaration/judgment and later alimentation administration). Daisy's creator page labels Aabenraa-Sønderborg Amt from 1933, while the archive overview gives the merger's effective date as 1 April 1932; treat the naming/transfer boundary as an archival catalogue issue to confirm with staff, not as settled from the catalogue label alone.\n- The county archive is the best starting point, but the civil/police records remain separate possibilities: the 1932 county merger did **not** transfer police or court powers to the amtmand. Rigsarkivet's archive creator note explicitly says amtmen had no police or court powers after 1919. Use the civil court and police route if the county file does not contain the judgment or source references.\n- The 1932 file is beyond the general 75-year access restriction described by Rigsarkivet, but availability of a particular file still needs confirmation in Daisy and with the archive.\n\n**Daisy catalogue check — no individual 1932 case reference surfaced online (researched 16 November 2026):**\n- Rigsarkivet's Daisy search instructions confirm that the search supports both archive creators and archive-series names, and that older terminology matters: *børnebidrag* was formerly called *alimentation*. Daisy recommends checking all series attached to a known creator and trying singular rather than plural series names.\n- Publicly indexed Daisy material confirms the archive creator **Sønderborg Amt** has 1920–1932 holdings, but the searches available here did not expose a 1932 *Alimentationsjournal*, *Alimentationssager* or individual case entry for Wilhelm Krogh. A Daisy search result for Sønderborg Amt showed 1920–1932 poverty-related series, but that is not evidence that the paternity file is in those series.\n- The official archive overview's *alimentationssager* description is a group-level description of the county journal archives, not a verified box or case number for this specific case. The printed finding aid by Hans Schultz Hansen (1998), pp. 11–86, is the next practical route for the Aabenraa/Aabenraa-Sønderborg material; the separate unprinted 1920–1935 finding aid and Sønderborg handover register must also be checked. No original case file or judgment has yet been located or read.\n- Practical request to the Aabenraa reading room: ask staff to search the 1920–1935 journal registers and journals for **Else Gedde Frederiksen (also Jensen), Wilhelm Krogh, Lorenz Heinrich Frederiksen, 5 April 1932 and 10 June 1932**, including the *alimentationssager* group and any cross-reference to the civil court. Ask them to check both Sønderborg Amt and Aabenraa-Sønderborg Amt, and the handover register. If the index uses a different spelling or a journal number, obtain the full case reference before ordering the file.\n\n**Jurisdiction trace — Gråsten, not Sønderborg (researched 16 November 2026):**\n- The official Rigsarkivet archive overview says that after the 1923 adjustment, **Egernsund (in Broager parish) was transferred into Gråsten judicial district**. A contemporary administrative summary likewise places Egernsund under the Gråsten police district. This resolves the earlier uncertainty about the local court/police jurisdiction for Else's Egernsund residence: start with **Retten i Gråsten / Gråsten Civilret** for the 10 June 1932 judgment, and **Gråsten Politikreds** for the initial police record. It does not establish which court decided the matter if Wilhelm Krogh lived in a different district; Rigsarkivet notes that where parents lived in different judicial districts, separate interviews and cross-references may exist.\n- Daisy's public catalogue lists Gråsten Civilret *Faderskabssager* only from **1962 onward** (and Gråsten Civilret *Retsbog for faderskabssager* from 1978 onward). Those named series therefore do **not** cover the 1932 judgment. The 1932 civil record may instead be in a general *sagliste* / *dombog* / *borgerlige sager* series, or may be referenced in the county's alimentation file. Do not order the post-1962 faderskab series expecting the 1932 case.\n- For police, Daisy lists **Gråsten Politikreds — Register til politiprotokol (1920–1959)** and a **Rejsepolitiprotokol (1932–1962)**. It also lists a *Politiprotokol for Lundtoft- og Nybøl Herred* (1923–1929), which ends before Lorenz's birth and is not itself the 1932 record. The register to the police protocol is the promising entry point; ask the archive which 1932 Gråsten police protocol or journal survives and whether the register covers the relevant case.\n- Rigsarkivet's current guidance says the civil route is a *Sagliste for faderskabssager* (or general civil *Sagliste*) followed by the corresponding *Dombog*. The Daisy results surfaced no Gråsten-specific faderskab series for 1932, so ask staff to identify the equivalent general civil series for 1932 and check the date 10 June. The judgment may also be cited by case number in the alimentation file.\n- Search terms remain Else Gedde Frederiksen/Jensen, Lorenz Heinrich Frederiksen, Wilhelm Krogh (Snogbæk), and dates 5 April, 10 June and 11 July 1932. The father's residence may point to a second district; confirm Wilhelm's exact 1932 address before treating Gråsten as the only court.\n\nOfficial guidance and finding aids:\n- [Rigsarkivet — paternity and adoption cases](https://www.rigsarkivet.dk/vejledning/faderskab-og-adoption/faderskabssager-kom-godt-i-gang/)\n- [Landsarkivet for Sønderjylland archive overview](https://www.rigsarkivet.dk/wp-content/uploads/2022/06/Landsarkivet-for-Soenderjylland-En-arkivoversigt.pdf)\n- [Daisy — Aabenraa-Sønderborg Amt archive creator](https://daisy.rigsarkivet.dk/arkivskaber_detaljer?a=&b=&c=b%C3%B8rnehjem&d=1&dir=a&e=2016&ed=&ep=&es=&f=&faid=&g=&gsc=he&h=&heid=14334077&henid=14334077&int=&m2rid=&meid=&ngid=709207&ngnid=&side=1&sort=3)","provenance":"Tidligere Lorenz-dossier, fuldt indarbejdet 16.11.2026","imported_on":"2026-11-16"},{"title":"Further catalogue tracing: 1932 Gråsten police paternity records","context":"16. Broager / Nybøl / Rinkenæs collateral records and name traps","text":"A second DAISY pass materially improves the police-archive route. The catalogue lists **Politiprotokol for faderskabsager, 1920–1976** under both:\n- **Aabenraa Politikreds / Åbenrå Politi** (creator's official period 1920–1973; catalogue series 1920–1976): [DAISY creator and series overview](https://daisy.rigsarkivet.dk/arkivskabers_arkivserier?a=&b=&c=politi&d=1&dir=a&e=2016&ed=&ep=&epid=&es=&f=&faid=&g=&gsc=he&h=&heid=&henid=&int=&m2rid=&meid=&ngid=723322&ngnid=723324&side=1&sort=3).\n- **Gråsten Politikreds** (the successor creator, officially 1973–2006; its catalogue nevertheless lists the same 1920–1976 paternity-protocol series, plus a separate *Faderskabsprotokol* for 1963–1976): [DAISY Gråsten creator and series overview](https://daisy.rigsarkivet.dk/arkivskabers_arkivserier?a=&b=&c=&d=&dir=a&e=&ed=&ep=&epid=&es=&f=&faid=&g=&gsc=&h=&heid=&henid=&int=&m2rid=&meid=&ngid=723236&ngnid=&side=1&sort=2).\n\nThis is stronger than the earlier generic Gråsten *Politiprotokol* / *Register til politiprotokol* route. The duplicated 1920–1976 series under both creators may reflect archival transfer or catalogue arrangement; it does **not** yet prove that a 1932 Gråsten case survives or identify its box/volume. Ask Rigsarkivet to check the 1932 section and determine whether Gråsten/Egernsund cases are indexed by mother's name, alleged father's name, or case number. The Gråsten creator overview also shows a separate *Faderskabsprotokol* only from 1963, so that later series is not the target.\n\nAabenraa's catalogue separately lists *Alimentationsjournal* (1920–1950), *Navneregister til alimentationsjournal* (1920–1950), and *Politiprotokol for faderskabsager* (1920–1976). These are additional leads, but Aabenraa's county/city jurisdiction should not be presumed to cover Egernsund. Ask whether the Aabenraa name register or journal cross-refers cases handled by Gråsten police, and whether the shared catalogue series is a merged or duplicated archival description.\n\nSources:\n- [Aabenraa Politics archive overview, DAISY](https://daisy.rigsarkivet.dk/arkivskabers_arkivserier?a=&b=&c=politi&d=1&dir=a&e=2016&ed=&ep=&es=&f=&faid=&g=&gsc=he&h=&heid=&henid=&int=&m2rid=&meid=&ngid=723322&ngnid=723324&side=1&sort=3)\n- [Gråsten Politics archive overview, DAISY](https://daisy.rigsarkivet.dk/arkivskabers_arkivserier?a=&b=&c=&d=&dir=a&e=&ed=&ep=&epid=&es=&f=&faid=&g=&gsc=&h=&heid=&henid=&int=&m2rid=&meid=&ngid=723236&ngnid=&side=1&sort=2)\n- [Gråsten Politics register and police-protocol catalogue listing](https://daisy.rigsarkivet.dk/arkivskaber_eller_arkivserie_liste?a=&b=&c=politi&d=1&dir=a&e=2016&ed=&ep=&epid=&es=&f=&faid=&g=&gsc=he&h=&heid=3914051&henid=3914051&int=&m2rid=&meid=&ngid=5195068&ngnid=5195086&side=1&sort=2)","provenance":"Tidligere Lorenz-dossier, fuldt indarbejdet 16.11.2026","imported_on":"2026-11-16"},{"title":"Wilhelm Krogh's 1932 residence: unresolved","context":"16. Broager / Nybøl / Rinkenæs collateral records and name traps","text":"This research pass did not locate an independently verifiable census or directory entry for Wilhelm/Vilhelm Krogh at Snogbæk in 1932. The birth-register marginal note's description “landarbejder Wilhelm Krogh, Snogbæk” remains the only presently documented residence clue in this dossier. Do not treat Snogbæk as proof that he was resident in Broager parish on the precise date of the declaration, nor merge him with the separate Carl Vilhelm Krogh 1908 candidate or Detlef Krogh 1905 candidate.\n\nThe exact address and employer remain important because Rigsarkivet's guidance warns that when the parents lived in different judicial districts, interviews and file references can be split across districts. The next useful work is to identify the relevant Krogh household in the 1930 census and contemporary parish/address records, then use that residence to test whether any second police/court district must be searched. No original paternity file has yet been located or inspected.","provenance":"Tidligere Lorenz-dossier, fuldt indarbejdet 16.11.2026","imported_on":"2026-11-16"},{"title":"County archive route now takes priority (1932)","context":"16. Broager / Nybøl / Rinkenæs collateral records and name traps","text":"Rigsarkivet's current guidance for 1919–1970 says the case began with police, but recommends searching the county archive first because its *Alimentationsjournal* and related file often contain the civil court's paternity case. The county file can be found through an *Alimentationsjournal*, an ordinary journal, and the corresponding *Alimentationssager/Journalsager*. Only if that route fails should the court and police be pursued. [Rigsarkivet: Faderskabssager – Kom godt i gang](https://www.rigsarkivet.dk/vejledning/faderskab-og-adoption/faderskabssager-kom-godt-i-gang/).\n\nFor Egernsund, the dates straddle the 1 April 1932 county-administration transition:\n- Sønderborg Amt archive covers 1920–1932.\n- Aabenraa-Sønderborg Amt archive covers 1920–1971; the official creator description explains that Aabenraa and Sønderborg came under the same amtmand in 1932 while retaining separate county council districts. The catalogue creator is officially named Aabenraa-Sønderborg Amt from 1933, but the archive's holdings begin in 1920.\n- The official archive overview identifies *Alimentationssager* as a normal county archive file group. It says the 1920–1935 journal-register/journal/file finding aid is unprinted and available in the reading room; the printed guide by Hans Schultz Hansen (1998), pp. 11–86, covers Aabenraa Amt 1920–32 and Aabenraa-Sønderborg Amt 1932–72. Sønderborg Amt's own pre-merger holdings have a separate unprinted handover list.\n- The 5 April 1932 paternity declaration and 10 June 1932 court decision therefore make it essential to check the transition-era Sønderborg and Aabenraa-Sønderborg finding aids, not simply assume one continuous county file.\n\nThe 1930 census is a practical way to identify Wilhelm Krogh's household/address: Rigsarkivet says Sønderjylland census records after 1920 are filed among the national rural-district/town census series and are available as scanned images through Arkivalieronline up to 1940. I have not yet found a name-indexed 1930 entry for Wilhelm Krogh/Snogbæk; this is an access route, not a claimed identification.\n\nPractical request for Rigsarkivet Aabenraa reading room:\n1. Consult the printed Schultz Hansen finding aid (1998), pp. 11–86, and the unprinted 1920–1935 journal-register/journal/file finding aid for the 1932 *Alimentationssager* and *Journalsager*.\n2. Check both Sønderborg Amt 1920–1932 and Aabenraa-Sønderborg Amt 1920–1971 holdings, paying particular attention to 1932 transition entries and whether the 5 April declaration, 10 June judgment or 11 July registrar letter is indexed under Else Gedde Jensen, Lorenz Heinrich Frederiksen, Wilhelm Krogh or a journal number.\n3. Ask whether the case file includes or cross-references the Gråsten Civilret decision and the Gråsten police investigation; if the county register has no entry, use the Gråsten paternity police protocol (1920–1976) and general civil registers.\n\nSources:\n- [Rigsarkivet paternity case guide](https://www.rigsarkivet.dk/vejledning/faderskab-og-adoption/faderskabssager-kom-godt-i-gang/)\n- [Rigsarkivet, Landsarkivet for Sønderjylland archive overview (PDF)](https://www.rigsarkivet.dk/wp-content/uploads/2022/06/Landsarkivet-for-Soenderjylland-En-arkivoversigt.pdf)\n- [DAISY: Aabenraa-Sønderborg Amt archive creator details](https://daisy.rigsarkivet.dk/arkivskaber_detaljer?a=&b=&c=b%C3%B8rnehjem&d=1&dir=a&e=2016&ed=&ep=&es=&f=&faid=&g=&gsc=he&h=&heid=14334077&henid=14334077&int=&m2rid=&meid=&ngid=709207&ngnid=&side=1&sort=3)\n- [Rigsarkivet census guide](https://www.rigsarkivet.dk/vejledning/folketaellinger-kom-godt-i-gang/)\n\nNo 1930 census identification, county journal number or original paternity case file has yet been obtained. These remain research targets, not findings.","provenance":"Tidligere Lorenz-dossier, fuldt indarbejdet 16.11.2026","imported_on":"2026-11-16"},{"title":"1930 census and correction to county search priority (16 November 2026)","context":"16. Broager / Nybøl / Rinkenæs collateral records and name traps","text":"A fresh check of Rigsarkivet's current paternity guide corrects the previous emphasis on the mother's Egernsund county archive. For cases of this period, Rigsarkivet says the paternity case was generally handled in the **county where the alleged father lived**; only when the father's name/address is unknown does it recommend starting with the mother's county. Thus Wilhelm Krogh's residence is the key jurisdictional fact. If Snogbæk is confirmed as his residence in 1932, the Sønderborg county archive remains the likely county route; if the 1930/1932 address points elsewhere, the relevant father's county must be checked instead. The county transition issue (Sønderborg Amt versus Aabenraa-Sønderborg Amt after 1 April 1932) matters only after the father's county is established.\n\nThe 1930 census route is real but not yet completed:\n- Arkivalieronline has a dedicated **Folketællinger, Sønderjylland** collection and the Landsarkivet for Sønderjylland's original census series (1769–1935). The series index is image-based; the online catalogue does not provide a name-searchable transcription for the target household.\n- Independent local-history/genealogy indexes cite the **5 November 1930 census for Broager Sogn, Nybøl Herred, Sønderborg Amt**, including household/family references such as *skema 82, familie nr. 1*. This confirms that a Broager 1930 census exists and is cited by researchers, but does not identify Wilhelm Krogh.\n- Search variants should include Wilhelm/Vilhelm, Carl Vilhelm, Krogh/Krog, and the place Snogbæk/Snogbaek. The candidate names already noted elsewhere in this dossier remain unmerged until the household and birth details match.\n\nPractical next step: inspect the 5 November 1930 Broager census by locality/household, starting with Snogbæk, then compare any Krogh entry with the 1908 birth candidates and with the birth-register marginal note. If there is no matching household in Broager, widen to nearby Sottrup, Nybøl, Dybbøl and Gråsten, and then to the county implied by any alternative address.\n\nSources:\n- [Rigsarkivet: Faderskabssager – Kom godt i gang](https://www.rigsarkivet.dk/vejledning/faderskab-og-adoption/faderskabssager-kom-godt-i-gang/) (explicitly says the case was generally handled in the father's county; mother's county is the fallback if father is unknown).\n- [Arkivalieronline: Folketællinger, Sønderjylland](https://arkivalieronline.rigsarkivet.dk/da/other/other-collection/48)\n- [Arkivalieronline: Landsarkivet for Sønderjylland, Originale folketællinger 1769–1935](https://arkivalieronline.rigsarkivet.dk/da/other/index-creator/48/14962499/20597409)\n- [Broager Sogn overview and census guide](https://wiki.slaegt.dk/index.php/Broager_Sogn)\n- [Local genealogy index citing the 1930 Broager census, skema 82](https://www.stroemming.dk/genealogy/gedweb/Public/da/Lists/placename-I.html)\n\nNo Krogh household has yet been identified in the 1930 census, and no exact address or employer has been established. The local genealogy index is a navigation aid, not evidence for Wilhelm's identity.","provenance":"Tidligere Lorenz-dossier, fuldt indarbejdet 16.11.2026","imported_on":"2026-11-16"},{"title":"Correct Arkivalieronline route for the 1930 Broager census (16 November 2026)","context":"16. Broager / Nybøl / Rinkenæs collateral records and name traps","text":"The previous census note pointed to the Sønderjylland special collection and the Landsarkivet's *Originale folketællinger* index. Rigsarkivet's own census guide gives a more precise route for this period: **after 1920, Sønderjylland's censuses are filed in the national census series under rural districts (*landdistrikter*) and market towns (*købstæder*)**, not in the special pre-1920 Sønderjylland census collection. The 1930 national series includes a dedicated *Folketælling 1930, Sognelister* selection page with fields for Amt, Kommune/Herred and Sogn.\n\nUse this route:\n1. Open [Arkivalieronline — Folketælling 1930, Sognelister](https://arkivalieronline.rigsarkivet.dk/da/rif/select/7/16972563).\n2. Select **Sønderborg Amt** → **Nybøl Herred** (or the corresponding kommune/herred option offered by the selector) → **Broager Sogn**.\n3. Inspect the schedule images locality by locality, beginning with Snogbæk; search handwritten spellings and the variants Wilhelm/Vilhelm, Carl Vilhelm, Krogh/Krog.\n4. If Broager is negative, repeat for the nearby parishes indicated by the place-name evidence rather than assuming Snogbæk belongs to Broager parish.\n\nThe selector page is a navigation form, not a searchable name index; no Wilhelm Krogh household has been read or identified yet. This corrects the earlier implication that the 1930 Broager material might need to be located through the Landsarkivet's special original-census index. The special collection remains relevant for older Sønderjylland censuses, but is not the preferred route for this 1930 search.\n\nSources:\n- [Rigsarkivet census guide — after 1920, Sønderjylland is under the national rural/town census series](https://www.rigsarkivet.dk/vejledning/folketaellinger-kom-godt-i-gang/) (section “Særlige forhold i Sønderjylland”).\n- [Arkivalieronline — 1930 parish lists selector](https://arkivalieronline.rigsarkivet.dk/da/rif/select/7/16972563).\n- [Arkivalieronline — 1930 census series listing](https://arkivalieronline.rigsarkivet.dk/da/rif/rif-collection/7).\n\nNo census household, exact address or employer has yet been verified; this update records the corrected archive route only.","provenance":"Tidligere Lorenz-dossier, fuldt indarbejdet 16.11.2026","imported_on":"2026-11-16"},{"title":"Follow-up: Sottrup census evidence and the 1932 Sønderborg court route (16 November 2026)","context":"16. Broager / Nybøl / Rinkenæs collateral records and name traps","text":"The new Vester Snogbæk identification is supported as a real place/parish combination by independent local census references. A published biography of Hans Jørgen Andresen explicitly cites the 5 November 1930 census for **V. Sottrup parish, Snogbæk**, held at Rigsarkivet. Strømming's genealogy index also cites 1930 census entries for several households in Snogbæk, Sottrup parish, including the Petersen household at matrikel Snogbæk 18. These references establish the correct census locality and that the 1930 schedules are used by researchers; they do **not** identify Wilhelm or his family. The public K-name index's only Krogh entry is an unrelated Anne Marie Krogh, so it is not a usable name index for Wilhelm.\n\nThe photo leads need to be kept separate:\n- Arkiv.dk search results list an undated item “1870–1900 Præstevænget 1, Nybøl, Detlef Krogh” and another “1927–1944 Detlef Krogh, Joller, Præstevænget 1, Nybøl”.\n- A local-history publication about Bachmann's brickworks names a Detlef Krogh among workers photographed in 1930.\nThese could be valuable visual leads, but the Præstevænget/Nybøl address is not the stated Vester Snogbæk address, and none of the image captions establishes whether the person is Wilhelm's proposed father (b.1876), brother (b.1905), or another Detlef Krogh. Do not use them to merge identities.\n\n**Court/police catalogue refinement:** a DAISY search for the exact series name “faderskabssager” finds the following Sønderborg-specific holdings:\n- Sønderborg Politi: *Journal vedr. faderskabssager* only **1944–1945**.\n- Retten for Sønderborg Købstad / Sønderborg Købstads Civilret: *Journal for faderskabssager* **1944–1957**, *Retsbog for faderskabssager* **1946–1957**, and later dedicated *Faderskabssager* series. These start too late for the 1932 decision.\n- However, DAISY lists a general **Retsliste for borgerlige retssager** for Sønderborg Købstads Civilret covering **1920–1957**. This is now the best catalogued court entry point for the 10 June 1932 decision: ask staff to search the 1932 retsliste by the names Wilhelm Krogh, Else Gedde Jensen and Lorenz Frederiksen, and then trace any case number to the corresponding general civil judgment/protocol.\n- The dedicated Gråsten *Politiprotokol for faderskabsager* is catalogued from 1920–1976, so it remains a possible source for the mother's-side investigation, even though the alleged father's proposed Sottrup address points to Sønderborg.\n\nThis confirms why the district split matters: the father-side 1932 civil case may be in Sønderborg's general civil court records, while interviews or police work concerning Else in Egernsund may be in Gråsten. Do not assume the later dedicated paternity series cover 1932 simply because the catalogue has a similar title for later years.\n\nNext action is to inspect the **1930 Sottrup parish schedules**, not Broager, and ask Rigsarkivet for the 1932 Sønderborg civil *retsliste* and associated judgment records. If the case is not in the Sønderborg retsliste, test the Gråsten general civil court registers as the mother's-side alternative.\n\nSources:\n- [Hans Jørgen Andresen biography, citing 5 November 1930 census at V. Sottrup, Snogbæk](https://denstorekrig1914-1918.dk/soenderjyder-oversigt/soenderjyder-a/andresen-hans-joergen-1891-1973/).\n- [Strømming: Kristen Petersen, Snogbæk, Sottrup — 1930 census citation](https://www.stroemming.dk/genealogy/gedweb/Public/da/Details/Details-I383.html) (unrelated household; locality confirmation only).\n- Arkiv.dk search lead: Detlef Krogh photo caption at Præstevænget 1, Nybøl (exact item page not recovered; no clickable URL until the catalogue record is identified).\n- [Local-history publication: Bachmann brickworks workers in 1930](https://slaegtsbibliotek.dk/2025/953685.pdf).\n- [DAISY exact-series search: faderskabssager](https://daisy.rigsarkivet.dk/arkivserie_liste?a=&b=faderskabssager&c=&d=1&e=2024).\n- [DAISY: Retsliste for civil cases, including Sønderborg Købstads Civilret 1920–1957](https://daisy.rigsarkivet.dk/arkivskaber_eller_arkivserie_liste?c=retsliste&d=1&e=2019).\n- [Trap 5: Nybøl Herred — court and police district changes](https://trap5.lex.dk/herred/nyboel-herred-soenderborg-amt/).\n\nNo Krogh household has yet been read in the original Sottrup 1930 census, and no 1932 court case number has been found. These remain the next record targets.","provenance":"Tidligere Lorenz-dossier, fuldt indarbejdet 16.11.2026","imported_on":"2026-11-16"},{"title":"Sottrup 1930 census search: access confirmed, no Krogh household yet (16 November 2026)","context":"16. Broager / Nybøl / Rinkenæs collateral records and name traps","text":"A fresh check of Rigsarkivet's current census guide confirms that **all Danish census material through 1940 is scanned and viewable on Arkivalieronline**. For Sønderjylland after 1920, the guide specifically directs researchers to the national census series under landdistrikter/købstæder. The correct 1930 series is *Folketælling 1930, Sognelister*; its selector has fields for Amt, Kommune/Herred and Sogn and explains that the schedules list households with names, ages, household roles, occupations and (from 1845) birthplaces.\n\nDirect route: [1930 census parish-list selector](https://arkivalieronline.rigsarkivet.dk/da/rif/select/7/16972563). Select Sønderborg Amt, Nybøl Herred and Sottrup Sogn. The selector is interactive and its selected image references are not exposed in the text-search index, so this research pass did not retrieve or read the Snogbæk schedule images themselves. **No claim is made that Wilhelm is absent from the original census.**\n\nIndependent locality cross-checks:\n- A biography for Hans Jørgen Andresen cites the 5 November 1930 census in Sottrup Sogn, specifically Vester Sottrup.\n- Strømming's public genealogy index cites 1930 census entries for Snogbæk, Sottrup Sogn.\n- Arkiv.dk search results identify a 1920–1930 image as “Snogbæk præstegård, Vester Snogbæk 6”. This corroborates the Vester Snogbæk place-name, not a Krogh residence.\n- Searches of indexed web material for “Wilhelm Krogh”/“Vilhelm Krogh” with Snogbæk or Sottrup did not surface a census entry. This is only a failure to find an indexed transcription; the original census is not name-searchable through the selector.\n\nA separate Strømming all-names index result for **Elisabeth Andersdatter** records her baptism at Sottrup in 1713 and death at Snogbæk in 1756. This is unrelated to the Krogh family but confirms Snogbæk as a historical locality within Sottrup Sogn.\n\n**Practical next step:** open the 1930 parish-list selector and inspect the Sottrup schedules image by image, locating the Snogbæk/Vester Snogbæk section. Transcribe all Krogh/Krog households and neighbouring farmhands, including variant first names. If the images are numerous, first locate the locality headings or schedule numbers using the 1930 Sottrup census citations in the local-history biographies, then inspect adjacent schedules. Follow any identified Wilhelm household back to the 1925 census and Adelby baptism register.\n\nSources:\n- [Rigsarkivet census guide](https://www.rigsarkivet.dk/vejledning/folketaellinger-kom-godt-i-gang/) — confirms scanned access through 1940 and the post-1920 Sønderjylland filing route.\n- [Arkivalieronline: 1930 parish-list selector](https://arkivalieronline.rigsarkivet.dk/da/rif/select/7/16972563).\n- [Hans Jørgen Andresen biography](https://denstorekrig1914-1918.dk/soenderjyder-oversigt/soenderjyder-a/andresen-hans-joergen-1891-1973/) — census reference for Sottrup, 5 November 1930.\n- [Strømming: Kristen Petersen, Snogbæk, Sottrup](https://www.stroemming.dk/genealogy/gedweb/Public/da/Details/Details-I383.html) — unrelated household, census locality reference only.\n- Arkiv.dk search lead: “Snogbæk præstegård, Vester Snogbæk 6” (exact item page not recovered; no verified item URL) — place-name/photo caption, not a Krogh record.\n- [Strømming all-names index](https://www.stroemming.dk/genealogy/gedweb/Public/da/Lists/allnames-A.html) — historical Sottrup/Snogbæk locality reference.\n\nNo Krogh household, 1930 address, 1908 baptism or 1932 court case number has been confirmed in this pass. The family-tree supplement's proposed parents and siblings remain secondary leads only.","provenance":"Tidligere Lorenz-dossier, fuldt indarbejdet 16.11.2026","imported_on":"2026-11-16"},{"title":"Revised first route for the 1932 paternity case: county archive before court (16 November 2026)","context":"Revised first route for the 1932 paternity case: county archive before court (16 November 2026)","text":"Rigsarkivet's current guidance for 1919–1970 explicitly recommends starting with the county archive: the alimentation/maintenance file often also contains the civil court's paternity case. If no county file is found, search the civil court; if that fails, search the police. This supersedes the earlier court-first ordering in this dossier. The case's recorded dates (declaration 5 April 1932, decision 10 June 1932, marginal note 11 July 1932) provide a narrow search window.\n\nFor the father's proposed 1932 residence at Vester Snogbæk, Sottrup Sogn, the first county target remains Sønderborg Amt. For the mother's Egernsund residence, keep the Gråsten-side locality and any cross-referenced police/court records in view. Rigsarkivet says that when parents lived in different judicial districts, interviews would typically occur in each district with cross-references. The 1932 administrative transition is a complication: the official archive overview lists separate Sønderborg Amt holdings for 1920–1932 and Aabenraa-Sønderborg Amt holdings for 1920–1971; the overview states the 1920–1935 finding aid for registers, journals and case files is unprinted and available in the reading room. The Daisy description for Aabenraa-Sønderborg Amt notes that the two counties came under the same amtmand in 1932 but retained separate county council districts, and that the amtmand's administration increasingly handled social and family-law matters including paternity. Do not assume that the new joint administration means the case must be filed under the later joint archive title: ask staff to check both the Sønderborg Amt and transition-era Aabenraa-Sønderborg holdings, with the 1 April 1932 boundary in mind.","provenance":"Tidligere Lorenz-dossier, fuldt indarbejdet 16.11.2026","imported_on":"2026-11-16"},{"title":"Reading-room request, prioritised","context":"Revised first route for the 1932 paternity case: county archive before court (16 November 2026)","text":"Ask Rigsarkivet Aabenraa to search the Sønderborg Amt and transition-era Aabenraa-Sønderborg county archive registers/journals for an alimentation or paternity matter concerning:\n- Child: Lorenz Heinrich Frederiksen, born 12 January 1932 in Egernsund.\n- Mother: Else Gedde Jensen, resident Egernsund (Gråsten judicial/police district).\n- Named/acknowledging father: Wilhelm/Vilhelm Krogh, landworker, resident Vester Snogbæk, Sottrup (Sønderborg judicial/police district).\n- Declaration: 5 April 1932; reported civil court decision: 10 June 1932; civil-register marginal note: 11 July 1932.\n- Search variants: Krogh/Krog; Wilhelm/Vilhelm; Else Gedde Jensen; Lorenz Frederiksen.\nRequest the journal number, the relevant Alimentationssag/Journalsag, and any enclosed copy or reference to the civil court decision. If the county register does not identify the matter, proceed to the Sønderborg Købstads Civilret general *Retsliste for borgerlige retssager* (1920–1957) for 1932, then the corresponding general civil judgment book and case file. The Daisy catalogue confirms that this general retsliste series exists for the relevant period; the dedicated Sønderborg court paternity journal and judgment-book series begin later and therefore are not the right 1932 starting point. Only after these should we request Gråsten/Sønderborg police registers and protocols.\n\nSources:\n- Rigsarkivet, “Faderskabssager – Kom godt i gang,” 1919–1970 route and cross-district procedure: https://www.rigsarkivet.dk/vejledning/faderskab-og-adoption/faderskabssager-kom-godt-i-gang/\n- Rigsarkivet, *Landsarkivet for Sønderjylland – En arkivoversigt*, county holdings and unprinted 1920–1935 finding aid: https://www.rigsarkivet.dk/wp-content/uploads/2022/06/Landsarkivet-for-Soenderjylland-En-arkivoversigt.pdf\n- Daisy, Aabenraa-Sønderborg Amt archive creator description: https://daisy.rigsarkivet.dk/arkivskaber_detaljer?a=&b=&c=b%C3%B8rnehjem&d=1&dir=a&e=2016&ed=&ep=&es=&f=&faid=&g=&gsc=he&h=&heid=14334077&henid=14334077&int=&m2rid=&meid=&ngid=709207&ngnid=&side=1&sort=3\n- Daisy, Sønderborg Købstads Civilret, *Retsliste for borgerlige retssager* (1920–1957): https://daisy.rigsarkivet.dk/arkivskaber_eller_arkivserie_liste?c=retsliste&d=1&e=2019","provenance":"Tidligere Lorenz-dossier, fuldt indarbejdet 16.11.2026","imported_on":"2026-11-16"},{"title":"Important distinction","context":"Revised first route for the 1932 paternity case: county archive before court (16 November 2026)","text":"The county archive search is a route to the administrative record and possible copy of the court ruling; it does not itself prove the biological father. The registered/legal paternity currently attributed to Wilhelm Krogh remains based on the civil-register marginal note and its references, while the family claim that Sophus Petersen was the biological father remains unproven until the original case and independent evidence are examined.","provenance":"Tidligere Lorenz-dossier, fuldt indarbejdet 16.11.2026","imported_on":"2026-11-16"},{"title":"Consolidation checkpoint: online evidence exhausted for the disputed 1932 paternity (16 November 2026)","context":"Consolidation checkpoint: online evidence exhausted for the disputed 1932 paternity (16 November 2026)","text":"I rechecked the online catalogue and indexed web sources for Wilhelm/Vilhelm Krogh in Snogbæk/Sottrup, the 1932 paternity decision, and the Adelby 1908 baptism. No indexed transcription of Wilhelm's 1930 household, no accessible 1932 case file or case number, and no digitised 1908 Adelby baptism entry surfaced. This is a research-access limit, not evidence that the records or people are absent.","provenance":"Tidligere Lorenz-dossier, fuldt indarbejdet 16.11.2026","imported_on":"2026-11-16"},{"title":"What is now firmly established as a research path","context":"Consolidation checkpoint: online evidence exhausted for the disputed 1932 paternity (16 November 2026)","text":"1. **1930 census:** Rigsarkivet's national collection lists “Folketælling 1930, Sognelister”; its selector has Amt, Kommune/Herred and Sogn fields. The Sottrup schedule must be selected and read as original images, focusing on Snogbæk/Vester Snogbæk. No searchable name index is exposed on the selector. The collection describes household-by-household enumeration with names, ages, household positions and occupations. Sources: https://arkivalieronline.rigsarkivet.dk/en/rif/rif-collection/7 and https://arkivalieronline.rigsarkivet.dk/en/rif/select/7/16972563.\n2. **1932 paternity:** The county-first request documented above is the appropriate archive route. There is no online evidence yet that identifies a case number or confirms the underlying ruling's full contents. The civil register's reported marginal note remains evidence of registered/legal paternity only; the family's claim for Sophus Petersen remains unproved.\n3. **Adelby 1908:** Archion's current public list for Adelby exposes “Taufen 1882–1907” (local signature Adelby_2.3), not 1908. It does expose the Adelby marriage register “Trauungen 1884–1937” (Adelby_5.3), accessible through an Archion account. The archive's separate overview describes a wider baptism series through 1949, so an archive enquiry is needed for the 1908 continuation. Source: https://www.archion.de/de/alle-archive/schleswig-holstein/landeskirchliches-archiv-der-evangelisch-lutherischen-kirche-in-norddeutschland/kirchenkreis-schleswig-flensburg/adelby/273644.\n4. **Nybøl Detlef:** The 1981 local-history register does list Detlef Krogh at holding no. 32, with the note “from 1883 only indersted.” It is a property-history lead, not an identification of the Detlef in Wilhelm's proposed family. The full PDF confirms the register title and that it is a Nybøl 1851 holdings list (printed p. 71): https://slaegtsbibliotek.dk/2025/953243.pdf.","provenance":"Tidligere Lorenz-dossier, fuldt indarbejdet 16.11.2026","imported_on":"2026-11-16"},{"title":"What would actually close the Lorenz branch","context":"Consolidation checkpoint: online evidence exhausted for the disputed 1932 paternity (16 November 2026)","text":"The decisive missing item is the original 1932 paternity/alimentation file or the court case file. It should settle what Wilhelm acknowledged, what the court decided, and whether any biological evidence or statements naming another man survive. The 1930 census and Adelby church book are supporting identity checks, not substitutes for that file. Until the county archive/court file is obtained, this branch cannot honestly be marked solved.\n\n**Triage decision:** online discovery for this branch is now exhausted enough that repeating broad web searches is unlikely to help. Keep it as a tightly specified archive retrieval task rather than letting it block the more promising ancestral lines. If the original case file is later obtained, revisit the biological-father question; otherwise retain the three distinct roles already documented (registered/legal father Wilhelm Krogh, family-claimed biological father Sophus Petersen, social father Peter Frederiksen) and do not collapse them into one.","provenance":"Tidligere Lorenz-dossier, fuldt indarbejdet 16.11.2026","imported_on":"2026-11-16"},{"title":"9. Lorenz Heinrich Frederiksen — explicit disputed parentage","context":"9. Lorenz Heinrich Frederiksen — explicit disputed parentage","text":"The current structured record for Lorenz (p6) has:\n\n- Mother: Else Gedde Jensen (p13).\n- Family-attributed biological father: Sophus (Sofus) Petersen (sofus).\n- Step-parent: Peter Frederiksen (pf12).\n- Spouse: Gerda Frederiksen (p7).\n\nThe Egernsund civil birth register (1932 no. 4, marginal note dated 11 July 1932) records Wilhelm Krogh (p12) as father following his paternity declaration of 5 April 1932 and a court decision of 10 June 1932. The family account says Sophus Petersen was the biological father and Wilhelm took responsibility. These claims conflict.\n\nThe record now carries two separate, explicitly disputed relationship claims:\n1. Sophus Petersen — family-attributed biological parentage.\n2. Wilhelm Krogh — paternity declaration and civil/court registration, disputed as proof of biological parentage.\n\nPeter Frederiksen remains a separate step-parent link. The tree should not collapse these three roles into a single “father” relationship.\n\nSources include the Egernsund civil register (AO, 1932 no. 4, image 125), Broager Vestre baptism entry (1933 no. 1, image 112), the family document *Paula Mathilde Chrestle Gedde og Jens Jensens slægt* and the handwritten family chart.","provenance":"Tværgående forskningsledger, indarbejdet 16.11.2026","imported_on":"2026-11-16"},{"title":"Official archival procedure verified for 1919–1970 cases","text":"Rigsarkivet's current guide states that from 1919 to 1970 paternity cases began with police notification or voluntary acknowledgement; if paternity was disputed, civil court decided it, and the county's alimentations case file often also contains the civil court paternity case. The recommended search order is county archive first (register to alimentations journal → journal → case file), then civil court case lists/judgment books, then police records. This materially sharpens the existing Aabenraa-Sønderborg Amt 1932 lead: search the 1932 county archive's register/alimentations journal and request the linked case file, then use civil court records only if the county file is incomplete. The 75-year access period has expired for a 1932 file. This official guidance confirms the route, not the contents or outcome of Lorenz's particular case.","provenance":"Direkte kontrol af Rigsarkivet og publicerede skifteuddrag, 17.11.2026","imported_on":"2026-11-17"},{"title":"Rigsarkivets officielle søgevej for faderskabssager 1919–1970","text":"Rigsarkivets aktuelle vejledning fastslår, at sager fra 1919–1970 typisk først bør søges i amtets arkiv: find register til alimentationsjournal og alimentationsjournal, slå moderens eller faderens navn op, og brug journalnummeret til at bestille den tilhørende alimentations-/journalsag. Hvis sagen ikke findes i amtsarkivet, er næste spor civilrettens saglister/dombøger og derefter politiets faderskabsprotokoller. Vejledningen siger, at journalsagerne ikke er skannet og skal bestilles til læsesalen. For 1932-sagen er det derfor rigtigt at begynde med Sønderborg Amt (og kontrollere Aabenraa-Sønderborg Amts arkivstruktur), men man skal først finde journalregisterets konkrete opslag. Den offentlige vejledning dokumenterer søgemetoden, ikke at Lorenz' sag er fundet.","provenance":"Rigsarkivets officielle vejledning 'Faderskabssager – Kom godt i gang' samt Rigsarkivets arkivoversigt for Landsarkivet for Sønderjylland; kontrolleret 17.11.2026. Der er ikke foretaget opslag i selve den personfølsomme sag.","imported_on":"2026-11-17"}]},"lorenz-christian-adam":{"person_ids":["p868"],"sources":[{"label":"Harbou: Slægten von Kleist i Danmark","url":"https://slaegtsbibliotek.dk/2023/938476.pdf"},{"label":"Vor Frelsers sogneinventar","url":"https://slaegtsbibliotek.dk/900379.pdf"},{"label":"Finn Holbek: Christian Adam von Kleist (sekundær registrering med DAA/Gotha-henvisninger)","url":"https://finnholbek.dk/getperson.php?personID=I22870&tree=2"},{"label":"Harbou, *Slægten von Kleist i Danmark* (1897), pp. 100–102","url":"https://www.v-kleist.com/FG_allg/Kleist_in_Daenemark.pdf"},{"label":"2025 Tychow-Dubberow family history, pp. 165–166","url":"https://www.v-kleist.com/FG/Dubberow.pdf"},{"label":"von Kleist family archive correction on Christian Adam","url":"https://v-kleist.com/FG/Muttrin/fg0283a.htm"},{"label":"Rigsarkivet's church-book release notes (2025)","url":"https://www.rigsarkivet.dk/wp-content/uploads/2022/02/Nyt-fra-de-frivillige-januar-2025.pdf"},{"label":"Rigsarkivet, guide to church books","url":"https://www.rigsarkivet.dk/vejledning/kirkeboger-borgerlige-vielser-m-m/kirkeboeger-kom-godt-i-gang/"},{"label":"Rigsarkivet, “Nyt på Arkivalieronline oktober 2025”, p. 1","url":"https://www.rigsarkivet.dk/wp-content/uploads/2022/02/Nyt-fra-de-frivillige-oktober-2025.pdf"},{"label":"Rigsarkivet, “Nyt på Arkivalieronline november 2025”, p. 10","url":"https://www.rigsarkivet.dk/wp-content/uploads/2022/02/Nyt-fra-de-frivillige-november-2025.pdf"},{"label":"2025 English machine translation","url":"https://v-kleist.com/FG/english/Dubberow_translation.pdf"},{"label":"Ahnentafel Peters, vol. 1","url":"https://dirkpeters.info/wp-content/uploads/2018/10/ahnentafel_peters_bd_1.pdf"},{"label":"H. W. Harbou, digitised original pamphlet (1897)","url":"https://slaegtsbibliotek.dk/2023/938476.pdf"},{"label":"Deutsche Biographie: Puttkamer family","url":"https://www.deutsche-biographie.de/gnd189411813.html"},{"label":"Fritz Mörre, “Die Swenzonen in Ostpommern”, *Baltische Studien*, N.F. 41 (1939), pp. 35–98","url":"https://dlibra.bibliotekaelblaska.pl/Content/69900/PDF%20czarno-bia%C5%82y/CZ.R.6.19czb.pdf"},{"label":"Deutsche Biographie: Valentin von Stojentin","url":"https://www.deutsche-biographie.de/sfz81519.html"},{"label":"NMBU thesis discussing Frederik Carl Eide's birth and his mother's 1815 marriage","url":"https://nmbu.brage.unit.no/nmbu-xmlui/bitstream/handle/11250/2648017/mif35.pdf?isAllowed=y&sequence=1"},{"label":"Pommerscher Greif: digitised heraldic literature, including Bagmihl's *Pommersches Wappenbuch*","url":"https://www.pommerscher-greif.de/forschung/literatur/heraldik/"}],"research_notes":[{"title":"2026-11-16 — Vor Frelsers 1705 baptism: archive route confirmed, entry not yet read","context":"2026-11-16 — Vor Frelsers 1705 baptism: archive route confirmed, entry not yet read","text":"A new source check clarifies the Vor Frelsers, Christianshavn, church-book structure. Rigsarkivet's 2025 Arkivalieronline release notes list the parish's *Enesteministerialbog*, content FVD 1686–1722. A separate published parish inventory explains that the minister and bell-ringer kept parallel registers before 1814. It identifies the minister's baptism books as F 1686–1813, volumes 1–5, and the bell-ringer's baptism books as F 1640–1813, volumes 12–19. The original baptism name index F 1704–1822 is volume 20 and points to the bell-ringer's baptism books 15–19 and 36.\n\n**Lookup sequence:** (1) search the bell-ringer's name index, volume 20, for Christian Adam around 1 October 1705; (2) follow its reference into the indicated bell-ringer's baptism book; (3) independently inspect the minister's FVD 1686–1722 register; (4) compare dates, names, parents and contextual details, preserving any disagreement between the parallel entries.\n\nThis confirms the *catalogue route and parallel-register structure*, not the baptism entry itself. No image, page number or Christian Adam entry has yet been inspected. Harbou's date and parentage remain published-genealogy claims requiring more evidence. No canonical relationship or tree data is changed.\n\nSources:\n- [Rigsarkivet, “Nyt på Arkivalieronline februar 2025”, p. 1](https://www.rigsarkivet.dk/wp-content/uploads/2022/02/Nyt-fra-de-frivillige-januar-2025.pdf) — lists Vor Frelsers Enesteministerialbog FVD 1686–1722.\n- [Sogn nr. 47, Vor Frelsers Sogn, archival inventory](https://slaegtsbibliotek.dk/900379.pdf) — describes the parallel registers and volume 20 index route; this is an inventory aid, not the baptism record.\n- [Rigsarkivet, guide to church books](https://www.rigsarkivet.dk/vejledning/kirkeboger-borgerlige-vielser-m-m/kirkeboeger-kom-godt-i-gang/).\n\n**Next:** inspect index volume 20 and follow its reference, then compare the minister's register. If the viewer does not expose the images, request the specific volumes from Rigsarkivet.","provenance":"Tidligere Lorenz-dossier, fuldt indarbejdet 16.11.2026","imported_on":"2026-11-16"},{"title":"Correction to the Vor Frelsers index access route (16 November 2026)","context":"2026-11-16 — Vor Frelsers 1705 baptism: archive route confirmed, entry not yet read","text":"Closer reading of the parish inventory's wording adds an important access caveat: it says the original name registers are all available **as copies in the reading room**. Thus volume 20 is a precisely identified index, but I have not established that the index itself is digitized on Arkivalieronline. The inventory identifies the bell-ringer's baptism registers as volumes 15–19; the minister's FVD 1686–1722 series is separately listed by Rigsarkivet's release notes. The practical next step is to ask Rigsarkivet whether they can consult or provide a lookup from name-index volume 20, and whether the relevant bell-ringer register volume is digitized or must be ordered. The previous lookup description should not be read as claiming that volume 20 is online. No baptism entry has been seen.","provenance":"Tidligere Lorenz-dossier, fuldt indarbejdet 16.11.2026","imported_on":"2026-11-16"},{"title":"Source audit — why the 1705 date and name still need the original entry (16 November 2026)","context":"2026-11-16 — Vor Frelsers 1705 baptism: archive route confirmed, entry not yet read","text":"A further check of Harbou's printed footnote and the von Kleist family association's online correction clarifies the provenance of the date:\n- Harbou states Christian Adam was born 1 October 1705 and baptised at Vor Frelsers, but his footnote 28 only discusses the older *Dansk biografisk Lexikon* error (which made the maternal grandfather Christian Casimir his father) and the variant “Christian Adrian” in the German family history. It does not give a parish-register page, archive signature or direct citation for the baptism.\n- The family association's correction for III.466 says that an archive search in Danish records produced the date 1 October 1705 and the second name Adam rather than Adrian. It does not identify the specific register, image, page or archive signature. This is an additional reported archival finding, but still not a directly inspectable citation.\n- The older DBL article gives 3 October 1705 and the wrong father. The 1 October date is supported by Harbou and the family association's report of Danish archival research, but the discrepancy can only be settled by reading the original entry or the relevant archival index and its referenced book.\n\nAccordingly, keep the date and parentage at **“Kræver mere belæg”**. Treat the family association's c.1960 archive-research statement as a provenance clue, not as a substitute for the underlying record. No canonical data changed.\n\nSources:\n- [Harbou 1897, Christian Adam and footnote 28](https://slaegtsbibliotek.dk/2023/938476.pdf).\n- [Familienverband derer v. Kleist, Ergänzungen zu III.466](https://v-kleist.com/FG/Muttrin/fg0283a.htm) — reports a Danish archive search but supplies no exact shelfmark or transcription.","provenance":"Tidligere Lorenz-dossier, fuldt indarbejdet 16.11.2026","imported_on":"2026-11-16"},{"title":"Update — the bell-ringer's 1704–1822 name index was released online (16 November 2026)","context":"2026-11-16 — Vor Frelsers 1705 baptism: archive route confirmed, entry not yet read","text":"**Correction to the access caveat above:** the earlier inventory says the original name registers are available as reading-room copies, but Rigsarkivet's later Arkivalieronline release notices show that the relevant index was subsequently digitised/released online.\n\n- October 2025 release notice: Vor Frelsers Sogn, *Navneregister til enesteministerialbog ført af klokkeren*, 1640–1822, content “FØDTE 1704–1749 m.m.”\n- November 2025 release notice: same series, content “FØDTE 1704–1822” and a second index “FØDTE 1750–1842”.\n- The parish inventory identifies the 1704–1822 index as volume 20 and says it refers to the bell-ringer's baptism books 15–19 and 36.\n\nThis supersedes the previous assumption that volume 20 was not confirmed online. The online release is now documented, but **the index's individual images have not yet been opened and Christian Adam has not yet been located in it**. The next step is to open the Vor Frelsers name-index series in Arkivalieronline, inspect the index under Christian/Christiern/Christian Adam (and possible Adrian spelling), note the exact page/image and book reference, then follow that reference to the baptism entry. Compare the minister's FVD 1686–1722 book separately.\n\nStatus remains **“Kræver mere belæg”** until the index and original entry are actually read. No canonical family relationship changes.\n\nSources:\n- [Rigsarkivet, “Nyt på Arkivalieronline oktober 2025”, p. 1](https://www.rigsarkivet.dk/wp-content/uploads/2022/02/Nyt-fra-de-frivillige-oktober-2025.pdf) — first listed release of the Vor Frelsers bell-ringer name index, 1704–1749.\n- [Rigsarkivet, “Nyt på Arkivalieronline november 2025”, p. 10](https://www.rigsarkivet.dk/wp-content/uploads/2022/02/Nyt-fra-de-frivillige-november-2025.pdf) — lists the expanded 1704–1822 index.\n- [Vor Frelsers parish archival inventory](https://slaegtsbibliotek.dk/900379.pdf) — identifies this as volume 20 and maps it to baptism books 15–19 and 36.","provenance":"Tidligere Lorenz-dossier, fuldt indarbejdet 16.11.2026","imported_on":"2026-11-16"}]},"lorenz-kleist-1848":{"person_ids":["p868"],"sources":[{"label":"Selmer: Nekrologiske Samlinger 1848–49, s. 68 og 114","url":"https://slaegtsbibliotek.dk/2023/938488.pdf"},{"label":"Harbou: Slægten von Kleist i Danmark","url":"https://slaegtsbibliotek.dk/2023/938476.pdf"},{"label":"Brejl: private personarkiver, gravbrev for Frederik Christian von Kleist","url":"https://brejl.dk/privatB.html"},{"label":"Kolding Stadsarkiv: Ingrid Bruuns kirkebogsafskrifter","url":"https://koldingstadsarkiv.dk/brug-arkivet/slaegtsforskning"},{"label":"Rigsarkivet: vejledning til kirkebøger","url":"https://www.rigsarkivet.dk/vejledning/kirkeboger-borgerlige-vielser-m-m/kirkeboeger-kom-godt-i-gang/"},{"label":"Kolding archival overview: older church-book inventory (C 469-3 and C 469-74)","url":"https://slaegtsbibliotek.dk/807141.pdf"},{"label":"Kolding Stadsarkiv: source collections","url":"https://koldingstadsarkiv.dk/kildesamlinger-2"},{"label":"Arkivalieronline: church books across Denmark","url":"https://arkivalieronline.rigsarkivet.dk/da/geo/geo-collection/5"},{"label":"Rigsarkivet: Nyt på Arkivalieronline marts 2026","url":"https://www.rigsarkivet.dk/wp-content/uploads/2022/02/Nyt-fra-de-frivillige-marts-2026.pdf"}],"research_notes":[]},"lorenz-kleist-stems":{"person_ids":["p868"],"sources":[{"label":"Muttrin 2025","url":"https://www.v-kleist.com/FG/Muttrin/Muttrin.pdf"},{"label":"Tychow-Dubberow 2025","url":"https://www.v-kleist.com/FG/Dubberow.pdf"},{"label":"Allgemeine Geschichte","url":"https://www.v-kleist.com/FG/Genealogie/AllgemeineGeschichte.pdf"},{"label":"Harbou 1897","url":"https://www.v-kleist.com/FG_allg/Kleist_in_Daenemark.pdf"},{"label":"English Tychow-Dubberow translation, p.123","url":"https://www.v-kleist.com/FG/english/Dubberow_translation.pdf"}],"research_notes":[{"title":"F. The separate Tychow-Dubberow → Danish Christian line","context":"7. von Kleist / von Woyten / von Stojentin ancestry","text":"Do not splice this line into the Borntin/Muttrin sequence:\n\n- The 2025 Tychow-Dubberow volume states that Georg II.55 of Groß-Tychow had one daughter, Hedwig Maria, who married Colonel Christian II.60; Georg died after 1645 and before 1665 and left no legitimate heirs. The same biography identifies Christina von Woyten as Georg's wife and her parents as Benedictus von Woyten and Elisabeth von Stojentin. Source: [Tychow-Dubberow English translation](https://v-kleist.com/FG/english/Dubberow_translation.pdf), PDF text around lines 3074–3075.\n- Harbou says Christian II.60 inherited an estate share from his father and another share through Hedwig Maria, but does not name the father in that paragraph. The 2025 Tychow-Dubberow genealogy gives the line **Bartes II.3 → Andreas/Drews II.11 → Carsten II.21 → Bernd II.39 → Christian II.60**.\n- The father of Reimer and Bartes remains disputed as set out above. That conflict affects the earliest Tychow bridge, not the later Bartes → Andreas → Carsten → Bernd → Christian sequence.\n- The 2025 Tychow-Dubberow volume is a revised family genealogy, but its translation states that the English text is machine-translated and not yet fully proofread. Check the German edition and the underlying Greifswald/Stettin records for critical details.\n\nThe Georg–Christina–Hedwig–Christian connection is well supported by the family-history source. It is a **different route** into the family from the Borntin/Muttrin route leading to Cartz Ulrik.","provenance":"Tidligere Lorenz-dossier, fuldt indarbejdet 16.11.2026","imported_on":"2026-11-16"},{"title":"Tychow / Reimer and Bartes: unresolved father conflict","context":"7. von Kleist / von Woyten / von Stojentin ancestry","text":"Two sources conflict:\n- The 2025 Tychow-Dubberow genealogy says Hans of Tychow I.24 had sons Reimer II.2 and Bartes II.3.\n- L. Quandt's transcription of the 13 April 1477 collective fief letter in *Allgemeine Geschichte* names “Pribbeslaw zu Muttrin mit seinen Söhnen, die Brüder Reymer und Bartes zu [Groß-]Tichow”.\n- The same general-history discussion says the earlier Tychow/Dubberow branch ancestor is not known from the older family records.\n\nTherefore the father of Reimer and Bartes is disputed. Do not state Hans I.24 as proven, and do not merge the Muttrin and Tychow reconstructions until the original charter/authenticated copy and the later genealogy's reasoning are checked.\n\nThe branch below Bartes is explicitly stated in the 2025 Tychow-Dubberow biographies as:\n**Bartes II.3 → Andreas (Drews) II.11 → Carsten II.21 → Bernd II.39 → Christian II.60.**\nReimer II.2's son is Bartez II.6; Bartez II.6 is not Andreas's father.\n\nSources:\n- [Muttrin 2025](https://www.v-kleist.com/FG/Muttrin/Muttrin.pdf)\n- [Tychow-Dubberow 2025](https://www.v-kleist.com/FG/Dubberow.pdf)\n- [Allgemeine Geschichte](https://www.v-kleist.com/FG/Genealogie/AllgemeineGeschichte.pdf)\n- [English translation, Tychow-Dubberow p.123](https://www.v-kleist.com/FG/english/Dubberow_translation.pdf)\n- [Harbou 1897](https://www.v-kleist.com/FG_allg/Kleist_in_Daenemark.pdf)","provenance":"Tidligere Lorenz-dossier, fuldt indarbejdet 16.11.2026","imported_on":"2026-11-16"},{"title":"High-value corrections from Harbou (1897)","context":"Additional reverse pedigree: 19th-century Danish side branches and medieval claims (16 November 2026)","text":"Harbou's article is especially useful because it explicitly warns that earlier Danish Kleist pedigrees by Benzon, Lengnick and others were often incomplete and inaccurate. It should be preferred over unattributed online pedigrees, while still being checked against the Danish records that Harbou cites.\n\n1. **Christian Adam III.466 is the son-in-law route, not a son of Christian Casimir.** Harbou identifies Christian Adam as the husband of Barbara Juliane von Kleist and names Cartz Ulrik von Kleist (III.369) as her husband. This supports the existing tree route Christian Casimir II.82 → Barbara Juliane → Cartz Ulrik → Christian Adam III.466. Harbou's note 28 specifically says the *Dansk Biografisk Lexikon* had incorrectly made Christian Adam a son of his maternal grandfather Christian Casimir; the family history calls him Christian Adrian. This is an explicit warning against importing that older error.\n2. **The supplied +4/+5 Danish descent does not match Harbou's account of Christian Adam's children.** Harbou lists Christian Adam's son Christian Frederik III.601, born 25 August 1743 and died 12 July 1799 at Bredsted, married Anna Margrethe Schubart. He does not list a son Frederik/Friedrich Carl Emil born 1742/43 and dying 1815. Nor does this Harbou branch support the supplied Christian Frederik Vilhelm (1774–1848), Sophie Frederikke (1776–1851) and Julie von Kleist (1814–1894) as descendants of Christian Adam. These names may belong to another Kleist line or reflect a conflation; do not attach them to p868 until their parents and source chain are established.\n3. **The 1813–1822 Copenhagen branch data in the supplied text appears to be misattributed.** Harbou places Margrethe Christine Elisabeth, Frederik Christian III.853, Holgerine Henriette Caroline, Christian Conrad Sophus, Aadamine Frederikke Christiane, Riise Sophie Emilie and Johan Eduard Valentin among the children of Valentin Ulrik III.726 and Anna Matthea Bierg — not as children or grandchildren of the alleged Christian Frederik Vilhelm line. Harbou gives:\n   - Margrethe Christine Elisabeth: born 25 August 1813 (not 25 March), died 9 March 1895.\n   - Frederik Christian III.853: born 8 October 1815, died 26 November 1892 in Copenhagen.\n   - Holgerine Henriette Caroline: born 17 April 1817, died 19 October 1845; married Hans Lindholm on **13 October 1838** (not 18 October).\n   - Christian Conrad Sophus: born 8 February 1820, died **28 August 1880** (not unknown).\n   - Aadamine Frederikke Christiane: born 2 August 1822, died 22 December 1874.\n   - Riise Sophie Emilie: born 7 February 1824, died 7 February 1864.\n   - Johan Eduard Valentin: born 2 December 1825.\n   The supplied list omits two of these children and shifts the branch's parentage. Harbou's source references include portraits and photographs at the Royal Danish Library; these may help identify the relevant individuals.\n4. **Herath/Herrath Juliane:** Harbou's text says she was a widow of Joachim Heinrich von Heydebreck at her father's death, was engaged in 1682 to a Baron von Borch, and later married a von Manteuffel. The second marriage is consistent with the supplied outline, but the proposed burial in Rendsburg on 31 October 1802 (or 1702) is not confirmed by the passage and needs a church-register check.\n5. **Cartz spelling:** Harbou's note 25 explains that Cartz in Danish records became Carl, Curt or even Lauritz, and suggests it is a shortened form of Carsten. Preserve these as variants rather than creating separate people.","provenance":"Tidligere Lorenz-dossier, fuldt indarbejdet 16.11.2026","imported_on":"2026-11-16"},{"title":"Established source anchor and limits","context":"7. von Kleist / von Woyten / von Stojentin ancestry","text":"The von Kleist Family Archive's Tychow-Dubberow translation, p.123, identifies Christina von Woyten's parents as Benedictus von Woyten and Elisabeth von Stojentin of Giesebitz, and identifies her earlier husband as Otto von Below of Pustamin. The relationship to Georg von Kleist is supported by the same family-history context. The exact wording and underlying references should be rechecked in the German edition before expanding collateral siblings.\n\nThe dossier's proposed Hans von Stojentin (1446–1517) as Elisabeth's father is described in the older notes as probable, not demonstrated. Do not promote it to an established parent link. The estate association with Giesebitz alone is not proof.","provenance":"Tidligere Lorenz-dossier, fuldt indarbejdet 16.11.2026","imported_on":"2026-11-16"},{"title":"Harbou's Christian II.60 (d.1679) and the Tychow sequence","context":"14. Detailed Muttrin extension register — exp020–exp042","text":"Harbou's 1897 passage (printed p.97 / PDF p.2) says Brandenburg colonel Christian von Kleist II.60 of Groß-Tychow inherited one part of the estate from his father and another part with his first wife Hedwig Maria von Kleist, daughter of Georg of Groß-Tychow and Christina von Woyten. His second wife was Barbara Eleonore von Kalckstein; he died in 1679. Harbou does not name Christian II.60's father in that paragraph. He calls Swedish cavalry captain Joachim Henning von Kleist II.59 his *Farbrodersøn* (paternal cousin), not half-brother.\n\nThe 2025 Tychow-Dubberow biographies instead explicitly give the chain:\n**Bartes II.3 → Andreas (Drews) II.11 → Carsten II.21 → Bernd/Bernhard II.39 → Christian II.60 → Christian Casimir II.82 → Barbara Juliane → marriage to Cartz Ulrik III.369.**\n\nThe same 2025 Tychow volume says Reimer II.2's son was Bartez II.6, while Bartes II.3's son was Andreas II.11. Bartez II.6 is collateral, not Andreas's father.\n\nHowever, the father of Reimer II.2 and Bartes II.3 remains disputed:\n- 2025 Tychow-Dubberow genealogy: Hans I.24.\n- L. Quandt's transcription of the 13 April 1477 collective fief letter: “Pribbeslaw zu Muttrin mit seinen Söhnen, die Brüder Reymer und Bartes zu [Groß-]Tichow”.\n- The same general-history discussion says the earlier Tychow/Dubberow branch ancestor is not known from older family records.\n\nDo not state Hans I.24 or Pribbeslaw as settled father until the charter/authenticated copy and the later reconstruction are reconciled. Also do not conflate Christian II.60 (Tychow numbering) with other von Kleist people bearing similar names or numbers in the Muttrin/Harbou numbering systems.","provenance":"Tidligere Lorenz-dossier, fuldt indarbejdet 16.11.2026","imported_on":"2026-11-16"},{"title":"Harbou worksheet and source-quality rule","context":"14. Detailed Muttrin extension register — exp020–exp042","text":"The uploaded 29-row Harbou reconciliation worksheet remains incomplete. It is an audit instrument, not proof. It must be filled against both the German original and Danish translation, with page images and exact names/dates. Harbou is a strong guide to Danish military generations and family relationships; for Pomeranian ancestry before 1700 it is a secondary reconstruction. Follow its citations to the German family history and Greifswald charters.\n\nSpecial checks:\n- p3473 “Esther von Kameke”: distinguish her from the unnamed N.N. von Kameke in the Geneanet-based parent proposal.\n- p3475 “Anna von Fürst”: confirm “Fürst” versus “Fürsten”.\n- p6949 Hedwig Maria von Kleist: verify the reported cousin-marriage context and estate brought into marriage.\n- p1736 Cartz Ulrik: independently check Harbou's reported capture/wounding at Helsingborg (1710) and Gadebusch (1712).\n\nSource anchors:\n- [Muttrin 2025](https://www.v-kleist.com/FG/Muttrin/Muttrin.pdf)\n- [Tychow-Dubberow 2025](https://www.v-kleist.com/FG/Dubberow.pdf)\n- [Allgemeine Geschichte](https://www.v-kleist.com/FG/Genealogie/AllgemeineGeschichte.pdf)\n- [Harbou 1897](https://www.v-kleist.com/FG_allg/Kleist_in_Daenemark.pdf)\n- [English Tychow-Dubberow translation, p.123](https://www.v-kleist.com/FG/english/Dubberow_translation.pdf)","provenance":"Tidligere Lorenz-dossier, fuldt indarbejdet 16.11.2026","imported_on":"2026-11-16"},{"title":"Independent re-check against the 2025 Tychow-Dubberow volume","context":"Genealogy-source stemma: 1555–1614 manuscripts and the Reimer/Bartes conflict (16 November 2026)","text":"The 2025 second edition of the Tychow-Dubberow biographies makes the Hans attribution explicit: its contents identify “the sons of Hans of Tichow (I.24)” as Reimer and Bartes; the biography says they were first cousins of Jürgen and Peter of Dubberow because their fathers were brothers. It then gives Reimer's wife as a von Born of Grassee and his sons as Bartez, Tessen and Joachim; Bartes's wife as a von Briesen and his children as Martin, Jacob, Andreas and a daughter who married Volze of Raddatz. This provides a more detailed published reconstruction of the Tychow branch and supports the later Hans tradition as the current family-association genealogy. Source: 2025 *Tychow-Dubberower Linie*, pp. 134–136, https://www.v-kleist.com/FG/Dubberow.pdf.\n\nHowever, the same volume's introduction says that the name Hans in the generation after the earliest period rests on later genealogical material. Its 1477 reference does not quote the father phrase; it cites the charter for Reimer and Bartes being brothers. The actual 1477 text in Urk. 125, as printed by Quandt, separately says “Pribbeslaw of Muttrin with his sons, Reymer and Bartes, brothers at Tychow.” Thus the 2025 volume's Hans genealogy is a published modern synthesis, not independent primary confirmation of Hans's paternity. It appears to preserve the later family-genealogy reading while the 1477 text as transcribed names Pribbeslaw. This reinforces the decision not to change the canonical parentage or present the issue as settled.\n\nThe 2025 volume also states that Tessen of Dubberow was a nephew of Hans of Tichow, while his father was allegedly Peter of Dubberow; it explicitly says the family records only state the nephew relationship and identify his marriage and two sons. This helps define the collateral relationship but does not resolve whether Hans or Pribbeslaw fathered Reimer and Bartes.","provenance":"Tidligere Lorenz-dossier, fuldt indarbejdet 16.11.2026","imported_on":"2026-11-16"},{"title":"13. Harbou / von Kleist reconciliation cautions","context":"13. Harbou / von Kleist reconciliation cautions","text":"The 29-row Harbou worksheet in the uploaded segment is an audit instrument, not a completed reconciliation. Four rows need special care:\n1. `p3473` “Esther von Kameke”: one proposed reconstruction names an unnamed N.N. von Kameke as wife of Hans Jürgen von Kleist, while another source says Anna Esther von Kameke was wife of Pribislaff III.281. Confirm whether these refer to the same woman or two different generations before assigning parents.\n2. `p3475` “Anna von Fürst”: verify whether the family name is “von Fürst” or “von Fürsten”.\n3. `p6949` Hedwig Maria von Kleist: Harbou says she brought part of Groß Tychow into her marriage; if both spouses were von Kleist from different branches, record the cousin-marriage relationship accurately.\n4. `p1736` Cartz Ulrik's capture/wounding at Helsingborg (1710) and Gadebusch (1712) is attributed to Harbou; independent military-history corroboration remains desirable.\n\nHarbou is valuable for the Danish generations and military service, but it is a secondary reconstruction for Pomeranian ancestry before 1700. Use it to locate the relevant German family-history entries and Greifswald charters, not as a substitute for them.\n\nA separate extension proposes Hans Jürgen von Kleist as father of Pribislaff III.281 (`p3472`) and an unnamed von Kameke as mother, via Geneanet reconstructions. This conflicts with the later exp020–exp042 Muttrin audit's source numbering and is not yet reconciled. Do not encode either parent in canonical data until the precise III.22 / III.54 / III.114 / III.186 → III.281 sequence is checked in the German Muttrin volume and source references.\n\nThe uploaded Tychow chain and Muttrin chain are not to be spliced. The 1477 Reimer/Bartes father conflict and the distinction between Pribislaff III.281 (d.1698) and Pribislaff III.22 (different person/branch) remain central safeguards.","provenance":"Tidligere Lorenz-dossier, fuldt indarbejdet 16.11.2026","imported_on":"2026-11-16"}]},"evald-niels-birthplace":{"person_ids":["p32"],"sources":[{"label":"Arkivalieronline: Register til Vor Frue Kirkes vielser 1814–1891","url":"https://arkivalieronline.rigsarkivet.dk/da/other/other-collection/29"},{"label":"Rigsarkivet: Kirkebøger – Kom godt i gang","url":"https://www.rigsarkivet.dk/vejledning/kirkeboger-borgerlige-vielser-m-m/kirkeboeger-kom-godt-i-gang/"}],"research_notes":[{"title":"1854-vielsen i Vor Frue: officielt vielsesregister fundet","text":"Arkivalieronline's official list of church-register finding aids explicitly includes **København Vor Frue Sogn — Register til Vor Frue Kirkes vielser 1814–1891**. This is the correct index to start with for the 1854 marriage already identified in the dossier. The register should provide the reference to the relevant marriage entry; then inspect the original main and duplicate parish registers and transcribe the groom's birthplace exactly, along with age, occupation, residence, parents and witnesses. The ambiguous reading “Hove/Høje/Høve” must remain unresolved until the original entry is read. The index is a finding aid, not proof of the birthplace or identity.","provenance":"Arkivalieronline official register listing and Rigsarkivet churchbook guide, checked 17.11.2026; the 1854 entry itself has not yet been read.","imported_on":"2026-11-17"}]},"evald-claus-probate-1811":{"person_ids":["p72"],"sources":[{"label":"Bogensholm Gods G298: skifteprotokol og register 1800–1811, Rigsarkivets Arkivalieronline","url":"https://arkivalieronline.rigsarkivet.dk/da/billedviser?epid=17175795"},{"label":"DS-Wiki: Bogensholm Gods, skifteprotokol G298 1800–1811 og Daisy/AO-links","url":"https://wiki.slaegt.dk/index.php/Bogensholm"},{"label":"Hanne Vinter Henriksen: Skifteuddrag Bogensholm Gods (G298), Mols Herred 1800–1811","url":"https://www.milloup.dk/hvh/download/skifter/bogensholm_gods_skifter.htm"},{"label":"Djurslandsdatabasen: Claus Gaardsted, født 1751, død 1811 i Bogensholm (sekundær database)","url":"https://djursslaegt.djursdatabasen.dk/efternavne/EFTERNAVNG.htm"},{"label":"Trap 4 (1925), Vistoft/Bogensholm, Claus Gaardsted og enkens auktion 1811","url":"https://netstol.dk/slaegtsForskning/trap_danmark_4/Trap_Danmark_4_udgave_%281925%29_Bind_6_Randers_Amt_Mols_og_OEster-Lisbjaerg_Herreder.pdf"},{"label":"Rigsarkivet DAISY: Djurs Sønder-Mols Herredsfoged, probate and auction series","url":"https://daisy.rigsarkivet.dk/arkivskabers_arkivserier?a=&b=&c=Djurs+S%C3%B8nde+herred&d=1&dir=&e=2022&ed=&ep=&epid=&es=&f=&faid=&g=&gsc=&h=&heid=&henid=&int=&m2rid=&meid=&ngid=522700&ngnid=2969153&side=&sort="},{"label":"Arkivalieronline: Djurs Sønder-Mols Herredsfoged, Justitsprotokol 1688–1919 (scanned)","url":"https://arkivalieronline.rigsarkivet.dk/da/other-collection/35"},{"label":"Danske Slægtsforskere, Skærsø Gods: skifte- og fæsteprotokoller","url":"https://wiki.slaegt.dk/index.php/Sk%C3%A6rs%C3%B8"}],"research_notes":[{"title":"Bogensholm G298 identificeret som den konkrete skifteprotokol; afgrænset negativt resultat i den publicerede afskrift","text":"En mere præcis arkivvej til Claus Gaardsteds skifte er nu identificeret. DS-Wiki's arkivoversigt for Bogensholm angiver en original skifteprotokol for Bogensholm Gods, 1800–1811, ved Rigsarkivet, med både protokol og register i Daisy samt en Arkivalieronline-enhed. Arkivoversigten linker desuden til Hanne Vinter Henriksens digitaliserede skifteuddrag for Bogensholm Gods (G298), Mols Herred 1800–1811.\n\nDen publicerede G298-afskrift har otte skifter. Den alfabetiske liste og de otte gengivne sager indeholder ikke en særskilt indførsel for Claus Gaardsted. Den sidste gengivne sag er Peder Andersen Tuesen i Bogens, 31. august 1811, fol. 15b. Dette er kun et negativt resultat i den publicerede afskrift; det beviser ikke, at Claus ikke blev skiftet. Den originale protokol og register skal kontrolleres, herunder om Claus' bo blev behandlet under en anden arkivskaber, i en auktionsprotokol eller som privat/ekstraordinært bo.\n\nKonkret næste kontrol: gennemgå Bogensholm G298-registeret 1800–1811 og derefter hele protokollen omkring 1811; søg også på enken Kirsten Marie Jørgensdatter og navnevarianter af Claus. Hvis der ikke findes en indførsel, følg auktionen i 1811 via Mols Herreds auktions-/tingbogsmateriale. Arkivalieronline-siden kunne identificeres, men originalbillederne er ikke læst i denne undersøgelse.","provenance":"Nye webkildekontroller 17.11.2026; DS-Wiki Bogensholm, Arkivalieronline og Hanne Vinter Henriksens G298-afskrift","imported_on":"2026-11-17"},{"title":"Uafhængig sekundær personpost for Claus Gaardsted","text":"Djurslandsdatabasens alfabetiske G-oversigt har en post: “Gaardsted, Claus, født 1751 i [ukendt], død 1811 i Bogensholm”. Det stemmer med projektets Claus Gaardsted (p72), hvis nuværende fødselsangivelse er ca. 1750, og med Trap 4/5's angivelse af hans død i 1811. Det er et nyttigt sekundært identifikationsmatch, men oversigten angiver ikke forældre, ægtefælle eller primærkilde på denne linje. Den kan derfor ikke afgøre Claus' fødested, første ægteskab eller børn.\n\nNæste trin er at åbne den individuelle Djurslandsdatabase-post (hvis den har yderligere kildehenvisninger) og efterprøve 1751/1811 i kirkebog og skifte-/auktionsmateriale. Bevar ca. 1750 i stamtræet, indtil original dåb eller anden samtidig kilde dokumenterer en præcis dato.","provenance":"Webkontrol af Djurslandsdatabasens efternavnsoversigt, 17.11.2026","imported_on":"2026-11-17"},{"title":"G298-uddraget 1800–1811 kontrolleret: intet registreret skifte efter Claus Gaardsted","text":"Den publicerede alfabetiske oversigt og skifteuddrag for Bogensholm Gods (G298), Mols Herred 1800–1811, er gennemgået. Den anfører otte sager: Dorthe Pedersdatter i Bogens (1800), Jørgen Pedersen Møller i Fuglsø (1802), Søren Mikkelsen i Bogens (1802), Jens Rasmussen Balle i Fuglsø (1803), Rasmus Nielsen Skomager i Bogens (1804), Dorthe Nielsdatter i Fuglsø (1804), Søren Rasmussen Kierre i Fuglsø (1803) og Peder Andersen Tuesen i Bogens (31.8.1811, fol. 15b). Claus Gaardsted står ikke i oversigten, heller ikke under en tydelig variant.\n\nDette er en afgrænset negativ kontrol af det offentliggjorte G298-uddrag, ikke et bevis for, at Claus ikke blev skiftet. Uddragets forfatter oplyser, at navne er standardiseret, og at teksten ikke er korrekturlæst; et skifte kan også være behandlet i en anden jurisdiktion, ved privat skifte eller i et auktions-/boopbudsforløb. Claus' død i 1811 og enkens auktion over Bogensholm samme år er fortsat dokumenteret i Trap 4/5, men selve skifteforløbet er ikke identificeret.\n\nKilde: Bogensholm Gods (G298), skifteuddrag 1800–1811: https://www.milloup.dk/hvh/download/skifter/bogensholm_gods_skifter.htm\n\nNæste skridt er at kontrollere original G298-register/protokol og søge i Mols herreds skifte-, auktions- og eventuelle private booptegnelser omkring 1811.
