Detailed Muttrin extension register — exp020–exp042","context":"14. Detailed Muttrin extension register — exp020–exp042","text":"This is the compact working crosswalk for the expanded research export. These IDs are not canonical people and the relationships remain research proposals unless the note below says otherwise. The 2025 Muttrin volume calls the early Stammtafel a *wahrscheinliche Anordnung* (probable reconstruction) based on contemporary documents plus later family records.\n\n| Research ID | Person / claim | Present evidence status |\n|---|---|---|\n| exp020 | Klest de Densin | Probable early ancestor in Quandt's reconstructed table; proposed 1289 reference and parentage of Prissebur require original citation check |\n| exp021 | Prissebur | 1364 Urkunde 83 appearance is reported by Muttrin 2025; parentage and sons are from later family genealogy/older letter |\n| exp022 | Henning M.3 | Relationship to Prissebur and son Henning M.8 relies on the 1576 family genealogy and later reconstruction |\n| exp042 | Henning M.8 | Added to the expanded JSON/GEDCOM after detecting that the previous export skipped this generation; said to be alive 1435 (Urk.104), husband of a von Wedelstett, father of Bisprow III.1 according to the old family genealogy |\n| exp038 | Unnamed von Wedelstett | Wife of Henning M.8 and mother of seven sons per old family genealogy; personal identity and maternity not independently proved |\n| exp024 | Bisprow III.1 | Muttrin 2025 explicitly says son of Henning M.8; cites 1477 enfeoffment and 1493 church-patronage evidence |\n| exp025 | Unnamed von Natzmer | Wife of Bisprow; maternity of his children is genealogy-reported |\n| exp026 | Hans III.6 | Reported youngest son of Bisprow; dated records 1477, 1493, 1498; death inferred from sons' 1516 enfeoffment |\n| exp027 | Unnamed von Bornentin | Wife of Hans III.6, daughter of Paul at Borntin per old family genealogy |\n| exp028 | Pribislaff III.22 | Borntin/Muttrin person; marriage to Dorothea Elisabeth von Below reported by Muttrin genealogy; don't confuse with Pribislaff III.281 / canonical p3472 |\n| exp029 | Dorothea Elisabeth von Below | Parents named as Gerhard Henning von Below and Catharina von Glasenapp in the family genealogy |\n| exp039 | Gerhard Henning von Below | Proposed father of exp029; no independent record checked |\n| exp040 | Catharina von Glasenapp | Proposed mother of exp029; no independent record checked |\n| exp030 | Hans III.54 | Son of Pribislaff III.22 per supplied notes; husband of Elisabeth von Kleist of Ruschitz |\n| exp031 | Elisabeth von Kleist of Ruschitz | Proposed daughter of Andreas of Ruschitz per family genealogy |\n| exp041 | Andreas von Kleist of Ruschitz | Proposed father of exp031; no independent record checked |\n| exp032 | Pribislaff III.114 | Son of Hans III.54 and father of Hans Jürgen III.186 per family genealogy; dated fief/church/patronage records cited |\n| exp033 | Hans Jürgen III.186 | Son of exp032; note says his son was canonical p3472 / Pribislaff III.281, but the structured parent fields do not encode this link |\n| exp034 | Unnamed daughter of Achatz | Wife of exp033; alleged daughter of Landrat Achatz III.110; no given name supplied |\n| exp035 | Achatz von Kleist III.110 | Proposed father of exp034; exact numbered biography/page crosswalk open |\n| exp036 | Jacob von Kameke of Körlin | Proposed father of Anna Esther von Kameke / p3473 in expanded research data |\n| exp037 | Catharina von Koller | Proposed mother of Anna Esther von Kameke / p3473 in expanded research data |","provenance":"Tidligere Lorenz-dossier, fuldt indarbejdet 16.11.2026","imported_on":"2026-11-16"},{"title":"Existing IDs and unresolved structural mismatch","context":"14. Detailed Muttrin extension register — exp020–exp042","text":"- Canonical `p3472` is Pribislaff von Kleist, husband of `p3473` Esther von Kameke; canonical parents are blank.\n- Expanded research export identifies `p3473` as Anna Esther von Kameke and proposes parents exp036/exp037.\n- The expanded note says exp033 (Hans Jürgen III.186) was father of Pribislaff III.281 / p3472, but the expanded JSON and GEDCOM structured father link were not populated.\n- A separate Geneanet-based extension proposes Hans Jürgen von Kleist as p3472's father and an unnamed von Kameke as mother. This is not reconciled with the Muttrin numbering. The Harbou worksheet flags p3473's “Esther” name as requiring verification because it may conflate two von Kameke women from different generations.\n- Do not add a parent link to p3472 or p3473 until the exact German Muttrin biography, the older III. numbering and the cited documents have been compared.","provenance":"Tidligere Lorenz-dossier, fuldt indarbejdet 16.11.2026","imported_on":"2026-11-16"},{"title":"1. What the 1575 and 1601 records actually establish","context":"1575–1618 fief-genealogy audit — the lost Muttrin descent account and later reconstructions (16 November 2026)","text":"Quandt quotes the concluding formula in the whole-family fief letters:\n\n- **1575, Urk. 502:** the persons listed in the Muttrin stem belong to that line “according to their credible account of descent presented to us” and are descendants of the late Prissebur Kleist of Muttrin.\n- **1601, Urk. 547:** repeats the same substance, calling the listed persons descendants of the late Prissebur, formerly seated at Muttrin, according to the account of descent submitted to the duke.\n\nThis is substantial contemporary legal evidence that, by 1575, the Muttrin branch had submitted a written pedigree/account tracing its descent to Prissebur. It is not the same as having that pedigree in hand: Quandt says the “Nachrichtung” survives neither in original nor copy. Its detailed sequence of intermediate generations therefore cannot be checked directly against the document that the ducal administration saw. The 1575/1601 letters support the branch-level descent claim to Prissebur, but do not by themselves independently verify every father-son link later printed in the family genealogy.","provenance":"Tidligere Lorenz-dossier, fuldt indarbejdet 16.11.2026","imported_on":"2026-11-16"},{"title":"2. The 1608 whole-family letter gives a three-stem classification","context":"1575–1618 fief-genealogy audit — the lost Muttrin descent account and later reconstructions (16 November 2026)","text":"Quandt quotes the formulae in Urk. 564 (1608):\n\n- first stem: the Muttrin line and its offshoots at Vietzow, Damen and Dargen, described as descendants of Prissebur Kleist of Muttrin;\n- second stem: the Dubberow line, described as descendants of Jacob Kleist of Dubberow and Tychow;\n- third stem: Vilnow and Raddatz, described as descendants of the elder Voltz Kleist of Vilnow.\n\nThis is an important continuity check: the 1608 fief record preserves the same broad three-part descent model as the 1477 “Oldervater with three sons” statement, and explicitly names Prissebur as the Muttrin-stem ancestor. It does not identify Prissebur with the 1289 witness or seal-owner, and it does not restore the lost pedigree submitted in 1575.\n\nA separate 1608 fief letter, Urk. 565, is described by Quandt as tracing the then-current Damen line by named intermediate generations to the 1477 brothers Peter, Bisprow and Churdt, sons of Dubschlaff. Quandt says the lines of the first and fifth brothers had died out. This is a more detailed branch-specific genealogy than the broad formula in Urk. 564; it concerns the Damen branch and must not be misapplied as a direct pedigree for Muttrin proper.","provenance":"Tidligere Lorenz-dossier, fuldt indarbejdet 16.11.2026","imported_on":"2026-11-16"},{"title":"3. The 1618 / 1621 / 1706 genealogies introduce a material contradiction","context":"1575–1618 fief-genealogy audit — the lost Muttrin descent account and later reconstructions (16 November 2026)","text":"The whole-family letters of **1618 (Urk. 585), 1621 (Urk. 594), and the compilation made in 1706 for the 1665 enfeoffment (Urk. 662)** rearrange the first, Muttrin stem into three sub-lines: Damen, Muttrin–Vietzow, and Ruschitz–Dargen.\n\nQuandt says the 1618 genealogy traces the other two sub-lines of the Muttrin stem through intermediate links from **Bisprow and Pribschlaff**. In the second major stem, called descendants of “Old Georg”, it traces the Dubberow line from Peter of the 1477 letter, but the Great Tychow line from **Reimer and Bartes as sons of Hans**.\n\nThat last assertion conflicts with the wording Quandt transcribes from 1477: the 1477 list calls Pribbeslaw of Muttrin the father of “the brothers Reymer and Bartes” at Tychow. The two statements cannot both be literal descriptions of the same immediate father-child relationship unless there is a copying/reading issue, an otherwise unrecorded Hans, or an error in one of the later reconstructions. Do not silently harmonize them.\n\nQuandt's source criticism is especially important here: he says this later “genealogization” in fief letters from 1618 onward was not independently worked out by the ducal chancery, which lacked the necessary information, but was apparently taken from Paul Bolduan's genealogical work. He says Bolduan also first names Georg as progenitor of the Dubberow stem. Bolduan's work was not yet in existence in 1608, so the earlier 1608 formulation must have come from another source. Quandt further notes that the surviving material submitted by the Damen branch for the 1608 enfeoffment (Urk. 563a) does not supply the data needed for the later genealogization; the source of the 1608 information therefore remains unknown.","provenance":"Tidligere Lorenz-dossier, fuldt indarbejdet 16.11.2026","imported_on":"2026-11-16"},{"title":"4. Revised evidentiary assessment","context":"1575–1618 fief-genealogy audit — the lost Muttrin descent account and later reconstructions (16 November 2026)","text":"| Claim | Current assessment |\n|---|---|\n| The Muttrin branch claimed descent from Prissebur by 1575 | **Strong, contemporary legal evidence**: explicit formula in Urk. 502, repeated in Urk. 547 |\n| A written Muttrin descent account existed in 1575 | **Strong evidence of existence**, but the account itself is lost |\n| 1608 grouped Muttrin/Vietzow/Damen/Dargen under Prissebur | **Strong evidence of the contemporary branch classification**, quoted from Urk. 564 by Quandt |\n| The Damen branch had a named intermediate pedigree to the 1477 brothers | **Documented as a claim in Urk. 565**, but the full text and source basis still merit direct re-reading |\n| Reimer and Bartes were sons of Pribbeslaw, as the 1477 wording says | **Explicit in the surviving 1477 copy as printed by Quandt** |\n| Reimer and Bartes were sons of Hans, as the 1618 genealogy says | **Later genealogical claim**, repeated in 1621 and 1706; conflicts with 1477 wording |\n| The 1618 genealogy independently proves the correct father | **No**: Quandt attributes the genealogization to Bolduan's compilation, not independent chancery research |\n| Prissebur of Muttrin = 1289 Prissebur / Klest de Densin | **Not established by these records** |\n\nThe key methodological point: the fief letters are valuable legal evidence that family members represented a descent and were admitted to joint fief rights on that basis. But their genealogical clauses are not all independent corroborations. Later letters repeat earlier family genealogies, and the pedigree document submitted in 1575 is lost. For the Reimer/Bartes question, the 1477 wording remains the earlier statement; the Hans attribution must remain a contradictory later tradition pending inspection of the underlying 1618 source and any Bolduan manuscript or surviving excerpt.","provenance":"Tidligere Lorenz-dossier, fuldt indarbejdet 16.11.2026","imported_on":"2026-11-16"},{"title":"Sources and limits","context":"1575–1618 fief-genealogy audit — the lost Muttrin descent account and later reconstructions (16 November 2026)","text":"- Quandt, “Die Bearbeitung der Genealogie des Kleist’schen Geschlechts,” *Allgemeine Geschichte*, pp. 45–47: https://v-kleist.com/FG/Genealogie/AllgemeineGeschichte.pdf\n- Kleist *Urkundenbuch*: Urk. 502 (1575), 547 (1601), 563a, 564–565 (1608), 585 (1618), 594 (1621), 662 (1706): https://v-kleist.com/FG/urkunden/Urkundenbuch_3.pdf\n- The analysis above is based on the printed charter editions as quoted and evaluated by Quandt. It is not a fresh palaeographic reading of the original fief charters or the lost 1575 “Nachrichtung”. The original 1477 parchment is also not extant in the edition's account; its text is transmitted by a sixteenth-century authenticated copy.\n\n**Canonical tree decision:** no parent edge added or altered. Keep the proposed Muttrin descent chain in the research dossier only, with the lost 1575 account and the Reimer/Bartes contradiction explicitly visible.","provenance":"Tidligere Lorenz-dossier, fuldt indarbejdet 16.11.2026","imported_on":"2026-11-16"},{"title":"The lost 1555–1560 “Nachrichtung” and Jacob Kleist's 1576 expansion","context":"Genealogy-source stemma: 1555–1614 manuscripts and the Reimer/Bartes conflict (16 November 2026)","text":"A closer reading of Quandt's §§ 14–17 materially refines the source history behind the early pedigree:\n\n1. **A Muttrin “Nachrichtung” existed by 1575.** The 1575 and 1601 whole-family fief letters explicitly say the Muttrin stem descended from the late Prissebur of Muttrin according to a written account submitted to the duke (Urk. 502 and 547). The account itself is lost.\n2. **Quandt dates that underlying account to c.1555–1560.** He argues that it covered the descendants of Bisprow and derived only the Muttrin stem from Prissebur, whom it placed around 1320. Quandt proposes that its author may have been the learned Pribslaw of Muttrin/Borntin (born c.1486, died 1570), formerly ducal/bishop's chancellor and cathedral scholastic. This authorship and the c.1320 placement are Quandt's reconstruction, not statements preserved in the lost document.\n3. **Jacob Kleist of Ruschitz expanded it in 1576.** Jacob was ducal chancellor and had access to the ducal archives, fief records, Marienstift foundation, and Colbatz monastery registers. Quandt identifies him as the author of the 1576 expansion and says he could consult the original and copies of the 1364 fief record during border disputes. This makes the 1576 compilation an unusually informed family source, but it remains a compiled genealogy and needs comparison with the cited documents.\n4. **A further redaction was made around 1590.** The surviving witness was copied by Kratz in 1863 from a Ruschitz estate-archive manuscript, later apparently lost. The copy has two mid-17th-century scribal hands and incorporates material from earlier redactions. Quandt distinguishes the 1576 original, a 1590 redaction, and a later c.1660 Ruschitz copy/manuscript. We do not possess the 1576 autograph.\n5. **Bolduan used Jacob's 1576 original.** Quandt argues that Paul Bolduan's Schema amplissimae nobilissimaeque familiae Kleistianae, composed in 1613 or 1614, was based on Jacob's 1576 genealogy and selectively carried some branches forward. It is therefore not an independent witness to the 1576 pedigree. The 1618, 1621 and 1706 fief-letter genealogizations appear to have been taken from Bolduan, not independently researched by the ducal chancery.\n\nThis source stemma is essential: a 1576 family account, Bolduan's 1613/14 schema, and the 1618/1621/1706 fief genealogies cannot be counted as four independent confirmations where they share a common textual ancestor.","provenance":"Tidligere Lorenz-dossier, fuldt indarbejdet 16.11.2026","imported_on":"2026-11-16"},{"title":"Reimer and Bartes: the conflict predates Bolduan","context":"Genealogy-source stemma: 1555–1614 manuscripts and the Reimer/Bartes conflict (16 November 2026)","text":"The 1477 fief letter, as transcribed by Quandt from the sixteenth-century authenticated copy, names **Pribbeslaw of Muttrin with his sons, the brothers Reymer and Bartes at Tychow** (Urk. 125). By contrast, the family genealogy text preserved in the later Ruschitz/Elzow tradition says that **Hans lived at Tychow and left two sons, Reimer and Bartes**, who received the joint fief after their father's death in 1477. The 1618 and later fief genealogies follow the Hans attribution.\n\nThis is not simply “1477 versus a 1618 invention”: the Hans version is present in the later transmission of the 1576/1590 family genealogy and thus may predate Bolduan. But Quandt cautions that the relevant sentences referring to a Stammlinea/Genealogia may be additions either to the 1576 source or to its 1590/1660 redaction; in his view the underlying 1576 narrative author did not know the name of Tessen's father and would otherwise have named him. The 1576 autograph is not extant, so the exact layer in which “Hans” entered cannot now be fixed.\n\n**Assessment:** the contemporaneous 1477 fief text remains the stronger evidence for the immediate father named there: Pribbeslaw of Muttrin. The Hans tradition is an early family-genealogy variant of uncertain textual date, not a reason to overwrite the 1477 statement. Possible explanations include a later insertion, conflation of generations or people, or an error in one of the transmitted texts; none is currently demonstrated. Keep the Reimer/Bartes father unresolved in canonical data and preserve both readings in the research dossier.","provenance":"Tidligere Lorenz-dossier, fuldt indarbejdet 16.11.2026","imported_on":"2026-11-16"},{"title":"What the 1576 account does and does not say about the medieval origin","context":"Genealogy-source stemma: 1555–1614 manuscripts and the Reimer/Bartes conflict (16 November 2026)","text":"Quandt's reconstruction says the lost 1555–1560 Muttrin Nachrichtung traced the Muttrin stem to Prissebur, one of three alleged stem-brothers, and set him around 1320. He explicitly says only the Muttrin stem was derived from Prissebur in that account; the connections of the other two stems to their supposed progenitors were conjectural. This is a much narrower claim than a proven, fully connected three-branch pedigree back to the 13th century.\n\nJacob's access to the 1364 fief document is a valuable provenance clue: the 1576 compiler may have been able to consult it directly. It does not prove that Prissebur in 1364 was father of a named Henning or that the later 15th–17th-century Muttrin line descends generation by generation from him. Those intermediate links still need their own documentary support.","provenance":"Tidligere Lorenz-dossier, fuldt indarbejdet 16.11.2026","imported_on":"2026-11-16"},{"title":"Archival retrieval targets clarified","context":"Genealogy-source stemma: 1555–1614 manuscripts and the Reimer/Bartes conflict (16 November 2026)","text":"- **Bolduan/Kiel copy:** Quandt says a copy made around 1735 by Christoph Kiel survived with Kiel's copied historical manuscripts, passed via Consistorialrat Brüggemann to the library of the General Landschaft of Altvorpommern in Stettin. The exact modern shelfmark has not been established in the material inspected. Request the Schema amplissimae nobilissimaeque familiae Kleistianae / Kiel copy from that library or its successor collection; do not assume it is the same as the Elzow file below.\n- **Elzow manuscript:** the family-archive overview identifies Stettin archive signature **65/16/0/60/498**, with the Kleist genealogy beginning around folio 180. The Polish archive catalogue describes this as a mixed genealogical manuscript volume dated broadly 1700–1800; it is not identified as Bolduan's Kiel copy. Online catalogue entry: https://www.szukajwarchiwach.gov.pl/en/web/guest/jednostka/-/jednostka/7907475?refererPlid=2144199.\n- **Bohlen manuscript:** Stettin archive signature **65/10/0/7/151**, titled Notizen zur Genealogie der Familie von Kleist. Manuskript Bohlen, has no scans and no content description. It is a separate research lead, not yet identified with Bolduan or Kiel: https://www.szukajwarchiwach.gov.pl/en/web/guest/jednostka/-/jednostka/7906489.\n- **Ruschitz manuscript:** Kratz's 1863 copy described by Quandt is the immediate accessible witness to the c.1590 redaction; the estate manuscript itself is reported lost. Search Kratz's printed transcription in §17 rather than treating it as the 1576 autograph.","provenance":"Tidligere Lorenz-dossier, fuldt indarbejdet 16.11.2026","imported_on":"2026-11-16"},{"title":"Canonical-tree decision","context":"Genealogy-source stemma: 1555–1614 manuscripts and the Reimer/Bartes conflict (16 November 2026)","text":"No parent edge is added or changed. The 1477 charter and later family genealogies conflict over the Reimer/Bartes father, and the 1576 autograph plus the lost 1555–1560 Nachrichtung are unavailable. The medieval Prissebur → later Muttrin sequence remains a research-only hypothesis until each intervening link is independently established. The existing canonical p3472 → p1736 (Pribislaff III.281 → Cartz Ulrik III.369) relationship is retained: Harbou's 1897 genealogy explicitly states it, but this audit has not produced a reason to alter it. No new person has been inserted into data/build_data.py.","provenance":"Tidligere Lorenz-dossier, fuldt indarbejdet 16.11.2026","imported_on":"2026-11-16"},{"title":"Sources","context":"Genealogy-source stemma: 1555–1614 manuscripts and the Reimer/Bartes conflict (16 November 2026)","text":"- Quandt, Allgemeine Geschichte, §§ 2–7 and 14–17, especially pp. 45–62: https://v-kleist.com/FG/Genealogie/AllgemeineGeschichte.pdf\n- Kleist Urkundenbuch, Urk. 125, 502, 547, 564–565, 585, 594, 662: https://v-kleist.com/FG/urkunden/Urkundenbuch_3.pdf\n- Polish State Archives, Stettin, 65/16/0/60/498 (Elzow and other genealogical material): https://www.szukajwarchiwach.gov.pl/en/web/guest/jednostka/-/jednostka/7907475?refererPlid=2144199\n- Polish State Archives, Stettin, 65/10/0/7/151 (Bohlen manuscript): https://www.szukajwarchiwach.gov.pl/en/web/guest/jednostka/-/jednostka/7906489\n\n**Limit:** this is a source-stemma analysis of Quandt's printed transcription and critique, not a fresh inspection of the 1576 autograph, Bolduan's original, Kiel's copy, or the original 1477 parchment.","provenance":"Tidligere Lorenz-dossier, fuldt indarbejdet 16.11.2026","imported_on":"2026-11-16"},{"title":"Muttrin medieval additions exp020–exp042","context":"7. von Kleist / von Woyten / von Stojentin ancestry","text":"A separate audit covers the 22 proposed people exp020–exp041 and the repaired missing Henning M.8 generation exp042. Key conclusions:\n- Prissebur's appearance in Urkunde 83 of 1364 is a dated documentary reference as reported by the 2025 Muttrin volume. His alleged parentage and sons are partly derived from a 1576 family genealogy and older letter; not all claims have equal evidence.\n- The 2025 Muttrin volume explicitly calls the early Stammtafel a probable arrangement based on both contemporary documents and later family records.\n- The notes say Henning M.3 was father of Henning M.8, who was alive in 1435 and, according to the old family genealogy, married a von Wedelstett and had seven sons including Bisprow III.1. The expanded export originally skipped M.8; it has been repaired by adding exp042 to the expanded JSON/GEDCOM and changing Bisprow's and the unnamed wife's links. These are still research-export data, not canonical-tree edits.\n- Bisprow III.1, Hans III.6, Pribislaff III.22, Hans III.54, Pribislaff III.114 and Hans Jürgen III.186 are reported in the 2025 genealogy with dated feudal, church, school and office references. Each parent-child link must be distinguished from what the underlying document actually says.\n- The expanded JSON note says Hans Jürgen III.186's son was Pribislaff III.281, existing canonical `p3472`, but the structured parent fields do not encode `p3472.father = exp033`. This narrative/structure mismatch remains open.\n- Existing `p3473` Esther von Kameke is identified in the expanded export as Anna Esther von Kameke, wife of Pribislaff III.281; the proposed parents are Jacob von Kameke of Körlin and Catharina von Koller. These are family-genealogy claims needing exact page/primary-record confirmation.","provenance":"Tidligere Lorenz-dossier, fuldt indarbejdet 16.11.2026","imported_on":"2026-11-16"},{"title":"Medieval-to-early-modern von Kleist evidence audit (16 November 2026)","context":"7. von Kleist / von Woyten / von Stojentin ancestry","text":"This is a source-by-source evaluation of the medieval and early-modern leads preserved in the archived branch dossier, cross-branch ledger, expanded export audit and the current family-history publications. It does **not** establish a continuous descent from the medieval Klest to canonical Pribislaff III.281 (p3472). The following evidence grades describe what each item supports, not the prestige of the family or the authority of a later pedigree.","provenance":"Tidligere Lorenz-dossier, fuldt indarbejdet 16.11.2026","imported_on":"2026-11-16"},{"title":"A-1. The earlier Conradus Clest and Bertoldus Clest (1248–1269)","context":"7. von Kleist / von Woyten / von Stojentin ancestry","text":"The 1248–1269 evidence concerns a separate, earlier Kleist-named pair in the Oder/Stettin region. It must be kept distinct from the 1289 Belgard witness group until a connection is demonstrated.\n\n- **Urk. 26 (1248):** Conradus Clest appears among witnesses to Barnim I's grant involving Tamzowa and Radekow. The Urkundenbuch cites the original in the Pommersches Provinzialarchiv, Schr. I, Chat. 1, Nr. 6. A later note says Conradus and Theodoricus Bawarus were still squires in 1248.\n- **Urk. 27 (17 June 1249):** the Colbatz monastery matricula gives “Bertoldus et Conradus Clest fratres” (brothers). This is an explicit relationship in the documentary tradition, although this edition is from the monastery's register rather than the original charter.\n- **Urk. 32a (1254):** a separate charter from the original then held in the Geheimes Archiv at Königsberg names “Conradus marscalcus et Bartoldus Clest fratres”. This is independent contemporary evidence for Conradus and Bartoldus as brothers.\n- **Urk. 48 (7 April 1269):** Conradus miles dictus Clest states that the village Belitz and associated holdings had been granted to him and his already deceased brother Bertoldus for life, with reversion to Colbatz after Conrad's death. The edition is from the Colbatz monastery matricula. This again explicitly identifies the brother relationship.\n- The 1251/1254 documents also place Conradus Clest in the ducal witness network; the 1254 record calls him marshal. This is a substantial documentary biography for this pair, but it is not a genealogical bridge to the later Muttrin family.\n\n**Assessment:** Conradus and Bertoldus are a well-attested sibling pair. The 1289 Urk. 75 witness phrase may refer to another pair/group of Clest brothers, as Sauer and von Ledebur argue, but no source found so far identifies those men as descendants of Conradus/Bertoldus. Conversely, no source proves they were unrelated. Keep both possibilities open and do not merge their families or assign the 1289 Clest to the seal-holder without further evidence.\n\nSource: [Urkundenbuch, Urk. 26, 27, 32a and 48](https://v-kleist.com/FG/urkunden/Urkundenbuch_3.pdf).","provenance":"Tidligere Lorenz-dossier, fuldt indarbejdet 16.11.2026","imported_on":"2026-11-16"},{"title":"A0. Critical source criticism: the 1289–1364 bridge is contested","context":"7. von Kleist / von Woyten / von Stojentin ancestry","text":"A targeted re-check of the printed primary-source editions and Eberhard Sauer's 1939 critique materially changes the confidence assessment in the first section.\n\n**Urk. 75 (27 January 1289):**\n- The document is a grant by Pribislaus, lord of Daber and Belgard, to Bukow Abbey of 200 hides near Persanzig. The witness list calls the witnesses the duke's *ministeriales*. The 1862 Kleist Urkundenbuch prints: “Nicolaus Hakenbeke, Johannes frater suus, Cristoforus cnuth, prissebur, clest fratres ... ministeriales nostri.” It says the original was in the Stettin State Archive, Kloster Bukow Nr. 6; the seal had fallen from its grey linen cords and the parchment was damaged in places. [Urkundenbuch, Urk. 75](https://v-kleist.com/FG/urkunden/Urkundenbuch_3.pdf).\n- The 1888 Pommersches Urkundenbuch edition prints the witness phrase with a comma after Clest as well: “Prissebur, Clest, fratres”. It identifies the source as the original in the Stettin State Archive, Kloster Bukow Nr. 6, and likewise notes substantial moisture damage and reconstructed text. [PUB III, Urk. 1489](https://kpbc.umk.pl/Content/23561/PDF/Pomorze_062_05.pdf).\n- The Kleist Urkundenbuch editor says the original has a comma between Prissebur and Clest, but not after Clest, and argues that *fratres* therefore refers to both men. Quandt develops this into the hypothesis that Prissebur and Clest were brothers, with Clest the progenitor.\n- Von Ledebur's alternative, preserved in Quandt's footnote, reads “Prissebur, [the] Clest brothers”: Prissebur is one witness, and *Clest fratres* a plural group/family. This would not identify the 1289 Clest as a brother of Prissebur.\n- Eberhard Sauer (1937 dissertation, published 1939) independently argues that the alternative is possible and that Kratz/Quandt's reading is not proven. Sauer notes that the 1248/49–1284 Conrad/Bertold Clest family appears in the Oder region, while the 1289 entry is in Belgard; the relationship of the 1289 Kleist(s) to Conrad/Bertold cannot be determined. Sauer also stresses the documentary gap between 1289, the 1325 Nycolaus, and 1364 Prissebur. [Sauer extracts, pp. 56 ff.](https://v-kleist.com/FG/Adel_Ostpommern.htm).\n- **Assessment:** the 1289 charter proves a witness entry containing Prissebur and Clest and establishes their status as ministeriales of Pribislaus. It does not securely prove that they were two brothers, that the second man's personal name was Klest, or that he was the same man as the seal-owner “Klest de Densin”. The family association's seal argument is corroborative but does not fill the parentage gap. Keep “Klest de Densin as progenitor” as a significant family-history hypothesis, not a demonstrated identity/parent-child link.\n\n**The 1325 Nycolaus Klest lead:**\n- A separate charter dated 13 November 1325 names “Nycolao Klest” among witnesses to a boundary settlement involving the Dargun monastery. The printed Mecklenburg/Pomeranian record says the text is from the original in the Hauptarchiv at Schwerin, Dargun collection, with the issuer's seal detached but preserved loose. The charter text itself does not state his residence, status, parents or relationship to the later Muttrin family. [Pommersches Urkundenbuch VI, Urk. 3888](https://kpbc.umk.pl/Content/23455/PDF/Pomorze_062_12.pdf).\n- Sauer suggests he may have been a citizen of Kolberg, but that is an interpretation, not wording in the witness list. Sauer treats him as an intervening name-bearing witness and explicitly notes the gap until Prissebur in 1364.\n- The Kleist association's Urkundenbuch notes this 1325 man as previously unknown to its older family history. **Assessment: independently documented Kleist-name bearer; no proven connection to the 1289 or 1364 people.** He is a genuine new research lead, not a link to insert into the pedigree.\n\n**Urk. 83 (22 June 1364):**\n- The Urkundenbuch prints the grant as dated 22 June 1364 and calls the man “Prissebur Klest ... von Muttrin”, holding the other half of 25 hides at Glyne/Glienke. Its source note is important: the editor used a 1589 transsumpt and several sixteenth-century copies, not the original 1364 parchment. [Urkundenbuch, Urk. 83](https://v-kleist.com/FG/urkunden/Urkundenbuch_3.pdf).\n- The current online “Wichtige Urkunden” summary mistakenly labels Urk. 83 as 1368, whereas the Urkundenbuch text, the document-to-person index and the 2025 Muttrin volume all give 1364. Urk. 84 is the 1368 Dubbeslaff Cleest item. [Online summary](https://v-kleist.com/FG/urkunden/urkunden.htm); [corrected index](https://v-kleist.com/FG/urkundenzuordnung.htm).\n- **Assessment:** the 1364 date and the wording identifying Prissebur with the name Klest and Muttrin are consistently supported by the documentary tradition, but the extant printed evidence for Urk. 83 is through a 1589 transsumpt and later copies. It documents a landholder and place association, not his father.\n\n**Updated confidence for the opening bridge:** Urk. 75 → Urk. 83 remains unproven. The 1325 Nycolaus Klest is a separate possible clue, but it does not bridge the gap. The 2025 Muttrin volume itself accurately characterizes the early-person arrangement as a “wahrscheinliche Anordnung” based on contemporary documents and later family records; this qualification must govern how the whole chain is displayed.","provenance":"Tidligere Lorenz-dossier, fuldt indarbejdet 16.11.2026","imported_on":"2026-11-16"},{"title":"A. Earliest documentary horizon: 1248–1289","context":"7. von Kleist / von Woyten / von Stojentin ancestry","text":"| Lead | What the source actually supports | Assessment |\n|---|---|---|\n| Bertoldus and Conradus Clest, 1248 | The family association's Urkunden index lists Urk. 26 ff. as documentary mentions of these name-bearers. | **Documented namesake(s), not yet linked.** No evidence found in this pass that identifies either as ancestor of the later Muttrin, Tychow or Danish branch. |\n| Prissebur and Clest, 1289 (Urk. 75) | Quandt's transcription of the 1289 Bukow Abbey land grant names the witnesses “prissebur, clest fratres” among Pribislaw's ministeriales. The family-history argument reads these as brothers Prissebur and Clest. | **Strong medieval clue, disputed interpretation.** The charter transcription is evidence that both names occur in the witness list. The kinship reading depends on punctuation/Latin usage and is expressly challenged by von Ledebur; Quandt argues against that alternative. It is not safe to present the brother relationship as an uncontested literal statement. |\n| Klest de Densin seal | The family history reports a seal matrix found in 1834, bearing “S(igillum) Klest de Densin” and the Kleist arms, dated stylistically around 1300. The original matrix's post-1945 whereabouts are reported unknown; casts survive in the family archive. | **Corroborating heraldic/topographical argument, not a surviving contemporary parentage record.** It strengthens the association of a Klest at Densin with the later family, but does not name his children. |\n\nQuandt's own text is particularly important because it shows the reasoning rather than merely repeating a pedigree: [English translation, section IV](https://v-kleist.com/FG/english/Kleist%20history%201289%20to%201477.pdf), especially the discussion of Urk. 75 and the alternative reading (PDF text around pp. 170–171); compare the [German original](https://www.v-kleist.com/FG/Genealogie/genealogie.pdf). The family association's [important-charters index](https://v-kleist.com/FG/urkunden/urkunden.htm) labels Klest de Densin the ancestor, but that is the association's synthesis, not a new independent witness.","provenance":"Tidligere Lorenz-dossier, fuldt indarbejdet 16.11.2026","imported_on":"2026-11-16"},{"title":"A1. Quandt's own three-period framework and the 1477 charter","context":"7. von Kleist / von Woyten / von Stojentin ancestry","text":"Quandt's methodological introduction (General History, pp. 45–46) should be treated as the controlling source hierarchy for this early pedigree. He divides the genealogy into three periods:\n1. **From 1477 onward:** the oldest surviving fief charter gives a “secure family tree”, although he immediately cautions that individual documentary differences and the insertion of several persons still require investigation.\n2. **From 1316 to 1477:** several persons bearing the family name appear, but genealogical links exist only for some; the combinations needed to form a pedigree have varying degrees of certainty.\n3. **Before 1316:** three men bearing the family name occur, but whether and how they belong to the family, and which other men are ancestors or collateral relatives, is the question to be investigated. The origin is not already established.\n\nThis is unusually explicit caution from the principal genealogist himself. It means the full 1289-to-1698 chain must not be presented as having the same status as the documented 1477-and-later tree.\n\n**What Urk. 125 (13 April 1477) actually says:**\n- The surviving edition was prepared from a sixteenth-century authenticated copy (auscultated copy) in the Landesarchiv, not from the original 1477 parchment. The edition also notes further transsumpts in the 1546, 1568 and 1621 fief letters.\n- The copy lists Bisprow of Muttrin with sons Hennig, Peter and Hans; Dubbeslaff of Damen with five sons; **Pribbeslaff of Muttrin with his sons, the brothers Reymer and Bartes at Tychow**; brothers Jurgen and Peter at Dubberow; Voltze at Vilnow; and Curt with his brothers at Raddatz.\n- It records that the Kleists testified before the duke that they descended from an “Oldervader” who had three sons, from whom respectively came the Muttrin/Damen, Tychow/Dubberow and Vilnow/Raddatz lines. This is a valuable contemporary family claim preserved in the 1477 legal instrument. It does not name the Oldervader or independently establish his parentage.\n- The wording of the 1477 copy is significant for the Reimer/Bartes dispute: it expressly places them as sons of Pribbeslaff of Muttrin. Quandt later notes that the 1618 genealogy instead places the Tychow line under Hans, and that this later reconstruction is repeated in 1621 and the 1706 compilation. This is a genuine source conflict, not a trivial spelling variant. The original 1477 instrument/copy chain and the later fief genealogies must be compared before choosing a father.\n- The 1575 and 1601 general fief letters describe the Muttrin line as descendants of the deceased Prissebur of Muttrin. Quandt says the underlying family “Nachrichtung” (genealogical statement) has survived neither in original nor copy, although its main content can be reconstructed from the later fief documents. This is an important missing source behind the Muttrin ascent.\n\nSources: [Urk. 125, 1477, in the Urkundenbuch](https://v-kleist.com/FG/urkunden/Urkundenbuch_3.pdf); [Quandt, genealogy method and three periods](https://v-kleist.com/FG/Genealogie/AllgemeineGeschichte.pdf).\n\n**Assessment:** 1477 is the strongest early family-level evidence for the three branches and their then-current membership. It is a documented legal assertion of descent from an unnamed common ancestor, not proof that the unnamed ancestor is the same person as Klest de Densin in 1289. The exact fatherhood of Reymer and Bartes is disputed between the 1477 copy and later family genealogies.","provenance":"Tidligere Lorenz-dossier, fuldt indarbejdet 16.11.2026","imported_on":"2026-11-16"},{"title":"B. The Muttrin bridge: 1364 through the fifteenth century","context":"7. von Kleist / von Woyten / von Stojentin ancestry","text":"1. **Prissebur of Muttrin, Urk. 83 (1364).** The family association's current charter cross-reference assigns Urk. 83 to Prissebur of Muttrin in 1364 ([document-to-person index](https://v-kleist.com/FG/urkundenzuordnung.htm)). The 2025 Muttrin volume quotes him as “Prißebur, der Klest heißt von Muttrin” and says he was the second son of Klest (M.1). The 1364 document is the dated anchor; the claim that he was Klest's son is a reconstruction from the early family genealogy of 1576 and an older letter, not something the 1364 wording alone proves. **Grade: strong dated person/estate evidence; parentage probable, not primary-record proven.**\n2. **Jarßlaff and Henning as sons of Prissebur.** The 2025 Muttrin introduction explicitly attributes these sons to the 1576 family genealogy and an older letter concerning Glineke. The older letter reportedly says Prissebur obtained the property for himself and in the name of his two sons. This is meaningful family-record evidence, but the original letter and the 1576 genealogy need inspection and provenance assessment. **Grade: plausible and specifically sourced to later family records; not yet independently verified.**\n3. **The 1389 and 1407 names.** The current document cross-reference assigns a 1389 entry to Pritzbor at Zatkow and identifies Henning of Muttrin in Urk. 100 (2 February 1407). The 2025 Muttrin introduction says the 1407 record names Henning M.8 as the son of Henning M.3; it also discusses Jarßlaff's sons Kurt of Damen and Henning of Bruskow. **Grade: dated documentary appearances are strong; the family relationships must be kept attached to the exact named person and charter, not inferred from similar names.**\n4. **Henning M.8 and the seven sons.** The 2025 Muttrin introduction says Henning M.8 was alive in 1435 (Urk. 104), married a von Wedelstett according to the old family genealogy, and had seven sons: Bisprow, Pribslaff, Dubbeschlaff, Jacob, Schir, David and Voltzen. The seven-child list comes from the old genealogy; it is not stated as a full seven-son list in the cited 1435 record. **Grade: mixed evidence; dated life evidence plus later pedigree for wife/children.**\n5. **Bisprow and the 1477 enfeoffment.** The 2025 Muttrin volume identifies Bisprow (III.1) as son of Henning M.8, alive in the 1477 record and deceased about 1493. The 1477 collective fief letter is a strong documentary anchor for named landholders and some stated son-groups. It does not by itself prove every earlier link back to Prissebur.\n\nThe 2025 Muttrin volume itself says the early arrangement of persons is probable and based on contemporary documents together with later family records. This is a critical qualification, not a minor disclaimer. See [Muttrin, 2025](https://www.v-kleist.com/FG/Muttrin/Muttrin.pdf), introduction (PDF text around pp. 3–7), and the [charter cross-reference](https://v-kleist.com/FG/urkundenzuordnung.htm). Note a date correction for our working notes: **Urk. 83 is indexed as 1364 for Prissebur of Muttrin; 1368 is Urk. 84 for Dubbeslaff Cleest.** Do not carry forward a 1368 date for Prissebur.","provenance":"Tidligere Lorenz-dossier, fuldt indarbejdet 16.11.2026","imported_on":"2026-11-16"},{"title":"B1. What the 1407 and 1435 documents actually establish","context":"7. von Kleist / von Woyten / von Stojentin ancestry","text":"The original-source editions give a useful distinction between dated appearances and the parentage supplied by later genealogy.\n\n- **Urk. 100, 2 February 1407:** the Urkundenbuch says the original was in the Pommersches Provinzialarchiv, Schr. XIX, Chat. 4, Nr. 65. It names the old Bispraw Clest; Henning Clest of Bruskow; Henning Clest of Raddatz; Henning Clest of Muttrin; Kurt Clest of Damen; Prybbeslaf Clest of Kresyn; and another Prybbeslaf, son of Henning Clest of Raddatz. They jointly guarantee for their cousin Clest, Henning's son of Raddatz, held prisoner at Neustettin. Eight seal straps are noted, two missing, with only an indistinct seal remnant on the last.\n- The record establishes a group of named Kleists, their estates/locations and a cousin relationship to the imprisoned Raddatz man. It does **not** state that Henning of Muttrin is the son of the earlier Prissebur, nor does it supply the full Muttrin parent-child structure. That link is supplied by the family genealogy.\n- **Urk. 103, c.1435:** a record of damage to the Teutonic Order's lands names Pribbeslaff Clest and Claws Kameke among men raiding Dąbczlaw and other villages. The edition says it comes from a foliant in the Königsberg State Archive. This is a dated appearance of a Pribbeslaff Clest in the regional conflict network; the record does not state his ancestry.\n- **Urk. 104, c.1435:** a letter from Duke Bogislaw IX to the Teutonic Grand Master, preserved in the Königsberg State Archive, says the Order's vogt had seized goods from “our man Henningh Klystes sone van mütteryn” (the son of Henning Kleist of Muttrin). The 2025 Muttrin genealogy identifies this Henning as M.8 and gives him as a son of Henning M.3. The document itself confirms the son and the Muttrin association but does not name the father in the way the later pedigree does; the identification with the 1407 Henning and the exact M.3 → M.8 link depends on the combined record sequence and family genealogy.\n- **Urk. 104a, 11 October 1436:** names Hermann Kleist as son of Curt Kleist of Damen, an explicit parentage statement in a separate collateral branch. It is useful evidence that the contemporary document book can preserve direct father-child wording, but it does not prove the Muttrin line.\n\n**Assessment:** the 1407 and 1435 records substantially corroborate the existence, geography and chronology of the people used in the Muttrin reconstruction. They do not, on their own, prove every link in the Prissebur → Henning → Henning → Bisprow chain. The distinction between a named person, a stated parent and a pedigree identification must remain visible.\n\nSource: [Urkundenbuch, Urk. 100, 103, 104 and 104a](https://v-kleist.com/FG/urkunden/Urkundenbuch_3.pdf).","provenance":"Tidligere Lorenz-dossier, fuldt indarbejdet 16.11.2026","imported_on":"2026-11-16"},{"title":"C. The 1477 charter and the three main lines","context":"7. von Kleist / von Woyten / von Stojentin ancestry","text":"Quandt's transcription of the 13 April 1477 collective fief letter (Urk. 125) says the enfeoffed Kleists testified that they descended from an “Oldervater” who had three sons. It then groups named people at Muttrin, Damen, Tychow/Dubberow and Vilnow/Raddatz. The [family association's charter summary](https://v-kleist.com/FG/urkunden/urkunden.htm) likewise says the letter confirms three lines from a common ancestor and his three sons.\n\nThis is important evidence for a **late-medieval family structure and shared agnatic tradition**, but it does not name the Oldervater or prove the exact parent-child links proposed by later genealogists. Quandt's transcription also contains a crucial ambiguity for the Tychow line: it reads “Pribbeslaff zu Muttrin mit seinen Söhnen, die Brüder Reymer und Bartes zu Tichow”; the 2025 Tychow-Dubberow genealogy instead places Reimer and Bartes as sons of Hans I.24. Keep that conflict open until the original charter or an authenticated copy is inspected.","provenance":"Tidligere Lorenz-dossier, fuldt indarbejdet 16.11.2026","imported_on":"2026-11-16"},{"title":"D. The Damen branch: Dubislaff “Bratta”","context":"7. von Kleist / von Woyten / von Stojentin ancestry","text":"The 2025 Damen volume says Dubislaff/Bratta (III.2), on Damen, was the son of Henning M.8 and Margarethe von Wedelstädt; it dates him to 1477 and death about 1482. It says the 13 April 1477 letter names him and five sons (Drews, Curt, Bisprow, Peter and Pribislaff), and that the sons were enfeoffed again in 1486 (Urk. 192). It also recounts his two marriages and assigns three sons to Catharina von Podewils and two to an unnamed von Glasenapp.\n\nAssessment:\n- The **five sons** have a particularly useful documentary route through the 1477 and 1486 enfeoffments.\n- Dubislaff's **parentage and wives** are presented in the 2025 family genealogy; inspect the cited old genealogy and original fief records before treating all details as independently established.\n- The account of his disputed acquisition of portions of Damen is a historical narrative about inheritance, pledges and family settlement, not proof of a direct line to p3472.\n- The Damen branch must not be assumed to be the same as the later Borntin line merely because both sit within the wider Muttrin-Damensche lineage.\n\nSource: [Damen volume, 2025](https://www.v-kleist.com/FG/Muttrin/Damen.pdf), especially the Dubislaff/Bratta biography (PDF text around pp. 2–4).","provenance":"Tidligere Lorenz-dossier, fuldt indarbejdet 16.11.2026","imported_on":"2026-11-16"},{"title":"E. The Borntin line and canonical Pribislaff III.281 (p3472)","context":"7. von Kleist / von Woyten / von Stojentin ancestry","text":"This is the critical bridge to the Danish officer Cartz Ulrik:\n\n- Harbou (1897) explicitly identifies **Cartz Ulrik (III.369)** as son of **Pribislaff (III.281)** of Muttrin, Borntin, Döbel and Drenow, and **Esther von Kameke**. Harbou says Cartz inherited Drenow from his father. This is direct secondary-genealogy evidence for the p3472 → Cartz Ulrik relationship and for the maternal name Esther von Kameke. Source: [Harbou, “Slægten von Kleist i Danmark,” pp. 95 ff.](https://www.v-kleist.com/FG_allg/Kleist_in_Daenemark.pdf), section II.a (PDF text around p. 101).\n- The family association's male-name register identifies Pribislaff III.281 as “auf Muttrin”, died 1698, with wife abbreviated “A. E. v. Kamcke” and a biography reference at FG III,3 p. 277 ([register search result](https://v-kleist.com/FG/Muttrin/register5.html); the broader name register also lists him among the Muttrin Pribislaffs). This supports the identity/date/estate and a wife whose initials are A.E.; it does not itself state his parents.\n- The current canonical tree has p3472 with no father or mother recorded, and p3473 named Esther von Kameke with no parents. Cartz Ulrik (p1736) is linked to p3472 and p3473. The 2025 Muttrin genealogy does give p3472's parents; that published claim should be evaluated and transcribed, not ignored, but should not be promoted to canonical structured parent links until its cited evidence and numbering crosswalk are checked.\n- The 2025 Muttrin family-history biography (III.281) identifies Pribislaff as son of **Hans Jürgen III.186** and a daughter of Landrat Achatz von Kleist of Vietzow (III.110). The expanded export contains the complete narrative and the exp033 record for Hans Jürgen, but the p3472 person record still has no structured `father` or `mother` field. Thus the relationship is explicitly asserted by the published family genealogy, but is not yet encoded as a structured parent edge and has not been independently verified from the original 1665/1699 enfeoffment records. A Geneanet tree makes a similar proposal; it is not independent corroboration.\n- The 2025 Muttrin biography identifies p3473 more fully as **Anna Esther von Kameke of Kratzig**, daughter of Captain Jacob von Kameke of Körlin and Catharina von Koller. The expanded export has these parents as exp036 and exp037, while canonical p3473 currently has no parents. This is a specific published genealogy claim, not an unsupported guess; nevertheless, it should be verified against the cited marriage/estate records before canonical parent links are added. Do not conflate Anna Esther with the separate unnamed daughter of Achatz who married Hans Jürgen.\n- The same expanded-export note reports a tradition from von der Osten that Pribislaff was beheaded following an “unlucky” duel. This is an anecdotal historical claim, not verified by a court or execution record; retain only as a sourced tradition, never as established fact.\n- The 2025 Muttrin note lists Pribislaff's children as Adam Heinrich (III.368), Cartz Ulrich (III.369), Bogislaff Henning (III.370), Hans Jacob (III.371), and daughters Anna Hedwig (married von Marunde), Catharina Elisabeth (married Adam Wilhelm von Kleist of Völzkow, II.83), and Barbara Agnise. The expanded tree currently only carries Cartz Ulrik as the child in the canonical line; the other siblings and their connections are collateral research leads.\n\n**Current assessment:** p3472 → Cartz Ulrik is a strong published-genealogy relationship. The 2025 Muttrin genealogy explicitly gives p3472's parents as Hans Jürgen III.186 and an unnamed daughter of Achatz III.110, and identifies his wife as Anna Esther von Kameke with named parents. These are well-defined secondary-source claims, not unsubstantiated guesses; the remaining issue is independent verification from the cited original records and consistent encoding in the expanded/canonical data. The 1698 death and A.E. von Kamcke identity are supported by the family association's register.","provenance":"Tidligere Lorenz-dossier, fuldt indarbejdet 16.11.2026","imported_on":"2026-11-16"},{"title":"The Muttrin inheritance chain behind Pribislaff III.281","context":"Kleist line to the Danish Gedde branch: direct chain and next proof targets (16 November 2026)","text":"The family association's *Materialzusammenstellung zu den früheren Gütern der Familie v. Kleist*, printed pp. 118–119, supplies a more concrete property-history chain:\n- In the second half of the 17th century, Muttrin passed from Hans von Kleist of Borntin (III.186) to his son Pribislaff (III.281).\n- Its footnote states that Hans III.186 inherited from his father III.114, who inherited from his father III.54, who inherited from his father III.22.\n- After Pribislaff's death, the division agreement dated **12 June 1698** allocated Muttrin to Christoph Jürgen III.470, son of his deceased brother Hans Jacob; Christoph Jürgen then ceded it to his cousin Adam Heinrich III.368, Pribislaff's eldest son.\n\nThis is valuable because it ties the numbered pedigree to a succession of named estates and inheritance events, rather than relying only on an online family tree. It is still a compiled estate-history summary, not the original fief or inheritance documents. The 12 June 1698 date here concerns the division of Muttrin; do not conflate it with the separately reported 12 July 1698 Drenow transaction. Those dates need checking against the originals.\n\nSource: *Materialzusammenstellung*, pp. 116–119, especially pp. 118–119: https://www.v-kleist.com/FG_allg/G_Zusammenstellung.pdf. Its introductory note says it is an evolving compilation of 18th- and 19th-century estate descriptions and cross-references the numbered biographies in the family history.","provenance":"Tidligere Lorenz-dossier, fuldt indarbejdet 16.11.2026","imported_on":"2026-11-16"},{"title":"H. Overall verdict and research order","context":"7. von Kleist / von Woyten / von Stojentin ancestry","text":"**What can presently be retained**\n- Medieval Klest/Clest persons are documented in the Pomeranian record tradition; the 1289 witness list and seal argument are a credible origin lead, but the interpretation of the witness relationship is contested.\n- Prissebur of Muttrin is documented in the 1364 charter tradition. His parentage and the Jarßlaff/Henning family sequence depend partly on later family records and reconstruction.\n- The 1477 fief letter is the key medieval bridge showing the late-medieval family structure and the three-line tradition.\n- The 2025 family histories and Harbou give useful, detailed Muttrin/Damen and Tychow-Dubberow branch structures, but the early generations are not uniformly proven by contemporary records.\n- Harbou strongly supports Cartz Ulrik as son of Pribislaff III.281 and Esther von Kameke. The identity of p3472 is clear enough to preserve; his parents are not.\n\n**What remains unproven**\n- A fully demonstrated biological chain from Klest de Densin (1289) to Pribislaff III.281 (d.1698).\n- Independent primary-record corroboration of the published parentage of Pribislaff III.281 (Hans Jürgen III.186 and an unnamed daughter of Achatz III.110), and correction of the missing structured parent edge in the expanded data.\n- Independent primary-record corroboration of Anna Esther von Kameke's identity and parents (Jacob von Kameke and Catharina von Koller).\n- The Reimer/Bartes father in the Tychow branch.\n- The proposed pre-1600 von Woyten and von Stojentin lines.\n\n**Next work, in order**\n1. Inspect Urk. 75 (1289), Urk. 83 (1364), Urk. 100 (1407), Urk. 104 (1435), Urk. 125 (13 April 1477) and Urk. 192 (28 December 1486) in the document book, recording exact wording, witness lists, seals, date and archival provenance. The family-history transcriptions are finding aids, not substitutes for the charters.\n2. Read the 2025 Muttrin biography around III.281 / printed p.277 in the German PDF, and crosswalk its current numbering against Harbou's III.281 and the old expanded-export IDs exp032–exp033. Resolve whether Hans Jürgen III.186 is truly p3472's father and identify the cited document(s).\n3. Inspect the 1576 genealogy and the older Glineke letter cited for Prissebur's sons. Establish where the originals are held and whether the quoted text survives.\n4. Compare the German 2025 Tychow-Dubberow volume with the English translation and inspect the original 1477 charter to resolve Reimer/Bartes.\n5. Locate and transcribe Zedler vol. 59's “Woyden/Woyten” article; trace every named person to its cited source rather than accepting the sequence as a pedigree.\n6. Search for primary records for the proposed Stojentin relationship at Giesebitz; do not use the estate as a substitute for a parentage record.\n\n**No canonical relationships were changed in this audit.** All medieval and early-modern proposals remain research assertions until the source work above is complete.","provenance":"Tidligere Lorenz-dossier, fuldt indarbejdet 16.11.2026","imported_on":"2026-11-16"},{"title":"4. Medieval von Kleist ancestry — unproven chain","context":"4. Medieval von Kleist ancestry — unproven chain","text":"The dossier proposes early figures including Klest de Densin (mentioned 1289), Prissebur (1364), Jarßlaff (sons reportedly mentioned 1407), Henning (alive after 1435), and Bisprow (enfeoffed 1477, died c. 1493), followed by later Muttrin-Damen-line figures.\n\nThe material mixes family numbering systems and does not establish a continuous evidenced chain to the later people already in the tree. Treat each generation separately and consult the original Muttrin-Damen family-history documents and cited footnotes. A surname, estate succession or noble-family association is not enough to establish biological descent.","provenance":"Tværgående forskningsledger, indarbejdet 16.11.2026","imported_on":"2026-11-16"},{"title":"Publication assessment — medieval and early-modern Kleist material","context":"Publication assessment — medieval and early-modern Kleist material","text":"The public family tree should include the credible historical evidence and the family-genealogy tradition, but should not draw an unbroken medieval pedigree where the sources do not establish one. The website overview is deliberately a narrative with explicit caveats; it does not create new parent-child edges.\n\n| Claim / person | Public status | What is established | What remains unresolved |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| Conradus Clest and Bertoldus Clest | **Well established as a sibling pair** | Urk. 27 (1249) explicitly calls them brothers; Urk. 32a (1254) and Urk. 48 (1269) independently support the pair. | No demonstrated connection to the 1289 Belgard witnesses or the later Muttrin stem. |\n| 1289 Prissebur / Clest witness entry | **Needs more evidence** | The charter names Prissebur and Clest among the lord's ministeriales. | Punctuation and the scope of *fratres* are disputed; neither a sibling relationship nor identity with the Densin seal-owner is secure. |\n| Prissebur Klest of Muttrin (1364) | **Well established as a named historical figure; descent unresolved** | Urk. 83 names Prissebur Klest of Muttrin and his holding at Glyne. | The edition relies on a 1589 transsumpt and later copies; it does not establish his parents or his connection to the 1289 entry. |\n| Muttrin descent from Prissebur | **Family genealogy; needs more evidence** | Fief records of 1575 and 1601 report a submitted descent account; Quandt reconstructs a c.1555–1560 account and Jacob Kleist's 1576 expansion. | The original account, 1576 autograph and the intermediate medieval pedigree are not extant/independently verified. Later fief genealogies are partly derivative. |\n| Reimer and Bartes at Tychow | **Needs more evidence; conflicting tradition** | Urk. 125 (1477) as transcribed names them with Pribbeslaw of Muttrin; later family genealogy and the 2025 Tychow-Dubberow volume place them as sons of Hans of Tychow. | The 1477 copy is not the original parchment; the later Hans reading may be an early insertion or transmission variant. Do not silently choose Hans or add a new canonical parent edge. |\n| Pribislaff III.281 (`p3472`) → Cartz Ulrik III.369 (`p1736`) | **Well established within the published Danish genealogy** | Harbou's 1897 published genealogy explicitly gives the relationship; it is already represented in canonical data. | Harbou is a secondary published source; this review did not locate a primary record that independently confirms the parentage. |\n\nThe 2025 Tychow-Dubberow volume is useful evidence of the modern family-association reconstruction, but it is not an independent primary witness to the disputed Hans paternity. The 1477 wording and the later Hans tradition should remain visible side by side. The source-stemma analysis and archive retrieval targets above remain active leads; publication does not close the research.","provenance":"Tidligere Lorenz-dossier, fuldt indarbejdet 16.11.2026","imported_on":"2026-11-16"}]},"lorenz-woyten-stojentin":{"person_ids":["p217"],"sources":[{"label":"Deutsche Biographie: Puttkamer","url":"https://www.deutsche-biographie.de/gnd189411813.html"},{"label":"Deutsche Biographie: Valentin von (1939)","url":"https://dlibra.bibliotekaelblaska.pl/Content/69900/PDF%20czarno-bia%C5%82y/CZ.R.6.19czb.pdf"},{"label":"Urkundenbuch, Urk. 26, 27, 32a and 48","url":"https://v-kleist.com/FG/urkunden/Urkundenbuch_3.pdf"},{"label":"PUB III, Urk. 1489","url":"https://kpbc.umk.pl/Content/23561/PDF/Pomorze_062_05.pdf"},{"label":"Sauer extracts, pp. 56 ff.","url":"https://v-kleist.com/FG/Adel_Ostpommern.htm"},{"label":"Pommersches Urkundenbuch VI, Urk. 3888","url":"https://kpbc.umk.pl/Content/23455/PDF/Pomorze_062_12.pdf"},{"label":"Online summary","url":"https://v-kleist.com/FG/urkunden/urkunden.htm"},{"label":"corrected index","url":"https://v-kleist.com/FG/urkundenzuordnung.htm"},{"label":"English translation, section IV","url":"https://v-kleist.com/FG/english/Kleist%20history%201289%20to%201477.pdf"},{"label":"German original","url":"https://www.v-kleist.com/FG/Genealogie/genealogie.pdf"},{"label":"Quandt, genealogy method and three periods","url":"https://v-kleist.com/FG/Genealogie/AllgemeineGeschichte.pdf"},{"label":"Muttrin, 2025","url":"https://www.v-kleist.com/FG/Muttrin/Muttrin.pdf"},{"label":"Damen volume, 2025","url":"https://www.v-kleist.com/FG/Muttrin/Damen.pdf"},{"label":"Harbou, “Slægten von Kleist i Danmark,” pp. 95 ff.","url":"https://www.v-kleist.com/FG_allg/Kleist_in_Daenemark.pdf"},{"label":"register search result","url":"https://v-kleist.com/FG/Muttrin/register5.html"},{"label":"Tychow-Dubberow English translation","url":"https://v-kleist.com/FG/english/Dubberow_translation.pdf"},{"label":"Tychow-Dubberow 2025","url":"https://www.v-kleist.com/FG/Dubberow.pdf"},{"label":"Allgemeine Geschichte","url":"https://www.v-kleist.com/FG/Genealogie/AllgemeineGeschichte.pdf"},{"label":"English translation, Tychow-Dubberow p.123","url":"https://www.v-kleist.com/FG/english/Dubberow_translation.pdf"},{"label":"Ahnentafel Peters, vol. 1","url":"https://dirkpeters.info/wp-content/uploads/2018/10/ahnentafel_peters_bd_1.pdf"}],"research_notes":[{"title":"1. Verified von Woyten / von Kleist additions","context":"1. Verified von Woyten / von Kleist additions","text":"The English translation of the von Kleist family history, *History of the Family von Kleist – Tychow-Dubberow line*, p. 123, states that:\n\n- Georg von Kleist of Groß-Tychow (died after 1645) was married to Christina von Woyten.\n- Christina was the widow of Otto von Below of Pustamin before marrying Georg.\n- Christina was the daughter of Benedictus von Woyten of Versin, princely Pomeranian captain of Bütow, and Elisabeth von Stojentin of Giesebitz.\n- Georg and Christina had one daughter, Hedwig Maria, who married Colonel Christian von Kleist (II. 60).\n- Georg's feudal estates passed to his son-in-law Christian.\n\nThe structured data includes Benedictus, Elisabeth and Otto, and records Christina's parents and her marriage to Georg. The first marriage to Otto is a separate union, not biological parentage.\n\n**Source:** [von Kleist Family Archive, English translation, p. 123](https://www.v-kleist.com/FG/english/Dubberow_translation.pdf).","provenance":"Tværgående forskningsledger, indarbejdet 16.11.2026","imported_on":"2026-11-16"},{"title":"G. Collateral Woyten and Stojentin leads","context":"7. von Kleist / von Woyten / von Stojentin ancestry","text":"These names are relevant to the wider family network but do not extend the medieval von Kleist male line:\n\n- **Christina von Woyten:** the 2025 Tychow-Dubberow volume says she was the widow of Otto von Below of Pustamin, daughter of Benedictus von Woyten of Versin and Elisabeth von Stojentin of Giesebitz, and wife of Georg von Kleist. It identifies Hedwig Maria as Georg and Christina's only daughter/only legitimate heir in the passage. This supports the named marriage/parentage claims as a published family-history account; recheck the German text and cited records before adding collateral children.\n- **Earlier von Woyten sequence:** the archived ledger proposes Michael → Conrad → Conrad Pantaleon → Vandemar → Carl Woiten (flourishing c. 1400), with Martin von Woyten as a great-uncle of Benedictus. No precise page/column references or transcriptions were supplied. **Unverified lead only.** The Zedler vol. 59 “Woyden/Woyten” article must be located and transcribed before any links are accepted.\n- **Earlier von Stojentin sequence:** Hans/Johann von Stojentin (1446–1517) is proposed as father of Elisabeth of Giesebitz, with Peter von Stojentin and Elisabeth von Lettow as his parents. The dossier itself calls this probable, and the estate association with Giesebitz is not parentage evidence. **Hypothesis only.**\n- Otto von Below is Christina's earlier spouse; do not make him Hedwig Maria's father.","provenance":"Tidligere Lorenz-dossier, fuldt indarbejdet 16.11.2026","imported_on":"2026-11-16"},{"title":"Von Woyten–Kleist descent: supplied pedigree and source audit (16 November 2026)","context":"Von Woyten–Kleist descent: supplied pedigree and source audit (16 November 2026)","text":"**Relevant existing tree IDs**\n- Christina von Woyten — `p13899`\n- Georg von Kleist, Groß-Tychow, II.55 — `p13898`\n- Hedwig Maria von Kleist — `p6949`\n- Colonel Christian/Carsten von Kleist, II.60 — `p6948`\n- Their son Christian Casimir, II.82 — `p3474`\n- Anna (Hedwig) von Fürst — `p3475`\n- Barbara Juliane von Kleist — `p1737`\n- Cartz Ulrik von Kleist — `p1736`\n- Christian Adam von Kleist, III.466 — `p868`\n- Otto von Below of Pustamin — `below_otto_pustamin`\n- Christina's recorded parents — `woyten_benedictus` and `stojentin_elisabeth`\n\nThe supplied pedigree is now a research lead attached to this dossier. The names, dates and relationships below are not all equally supported. The canonical tree has **not** been expanded or changed on the strength of the supplied compilation alone.","provenance":"Tidligere Lorenz-dossier, fuldt indarbejdet 16.11.2026","imported_on":"2026-11-16"},{"title":"What the 2025 von Kleist family history confirms","context":"Von Woyten–Kleist descent: supplied pedigree and source audit (16 November 2026)","text":"The 2025 revised *Tychow-Dubberow* history (English machine translation; German edition 2024) gives a strong published-genealogy framework:\n\n1. **Georg II.55 and Christina:** Georg is recorded as still a minor in 1609, taxed at Groß-Tychow in 1629, and holding shares in Mandelatz and Groß-Tychow in 1645. The compiler infers that he died after 1645 and certainly before 1665. His wife is named Christina von Woyten, widow of Otto von Below of Pustamin, daughter of Benedictus von Woyten of Versin and Elisabeth von Stojentin of Giesebitz. Their only daughter is Hedwig Maria. No birth or death date is supplied for Christina or Hedwig Maria. The book does not support estimating Christina's dates from her father's lifespan.\n2. **Hedwig Maria and Christian/Carsten II.60:** the colonel died in 1679. His first marriage to Hedwig Maria produced four sons — Bernd Erdmann II.81, Christian Casimir II.82, Adam Wilhelm II.83 and Sebastian Heinrich II.84 — and daughter Herath Juliana. His second marriage, to Barbara Eleonora von Kalkstein, produced a separate son and daughter. Keep the two marriages' children distinct.\n3. **Bernd Erdmann II.81:** died 26 December 1701 at Helsingør and was buried at St. Mary's Church. His wife was Elisabeth Hedwig von Schack. They had son Christian Wilhelm II.116 and four daughters: Juliana Loysa, Maria Elisabeth, Amalia and Charlotta.\n4. **Christian Wilhelm II.116:** born circa 1684 and died 1763. He married Anna Elisabeth von Kleist and Agnes Maria von Kleist. The book lists five sons — Bernd Dubslaff II.153, Ernst Ewald II.154, Rüdiger Christian II.155, Hans Sigismund II.156 and Joachim (Rüdiger) Wilhelm II.157 — and daughter Hedwig Elisabeth from the second marriage. The five sons' branch is described as ending with their childless deaths. Some individual dates in the supplied compilation need correction: for example, II.153 is given as born circa 1718 and died 1769 in the biography, but the same entry's narrative says he died 22 November 1756; this internal inconsistency needs checking against the cited source/records before reusing either death year.\n5. **Christian Casimir II.82:** born 1654, died 19 February 1722. His wife is named Anna Hedwig von Fürst, who died in 1722. The couple had one son, Andreas Wilhelm II.117, and four daughters, including Barbara Juliane, who married Lieutenant Colonel Cartz Ulrik von Kleist III.369. This is the crucial bridge to Christian Adam: he is not a son of Christian Casimir. The descent runs through **Christian Casimir → Barbara Juliane → her marriage to Cartz Ulrik → Christian Adam III.466**. The supplied list incorrectly places Barbara Juliane and Andreas Wilhelm as children of Christian Adam in its Danish-branch section; they are siblings/children of Christian Casimir in the earlier generation. The book says the male branch through Andreas Wilhelm later died out with his grandchildren; that does not erase the separate descent through Barbara Juliane.","provenance":"Tidligere Lorenz-dossier, fuldt indarbejdet 16.11.2026","imported_on":"2026-11-16"},{"title":"The Christina Woyten identity question: two Georgs","context":"Von Woyten–Kleist descent: supplied pedigree and source audit (16 November 2026)","text":"The same 2025 book preserves the unresolved point already noted in the canonical record:\n- Georg II.55 of Groß-Tychow (died after 1645) had a wife Christina von Woyten, widow of Otto von Below, and daughter Hedwig Maria.\n- Georg II.44 of Wendisch-Tychow/Crolow died in 1629. The book reports, following von der Osten, a first wife Christina Woyten of Versin, said to have borne him no children, and a second wife Dorothea von Below, who bore two sons. The compiler says other family records do not mention II.44's first wife. A note attached to the II.55 account says von der Osten mistakenly assigns II.55's Christina to Georg II.44.\n\nThis is **not** proof that one woman married both Georgs, nor proof that there were two women. The second account is explicitly dependent on von der Osten and is absent from other family records. A possible sequence involving II.44, Otto von Below and II.55 remains a hypothesis only. Do not merge identities, change Christina's spouse links, or create a second Christina node until the original cited source behind von der Osten and the relevant estate/marriage records are inspected.","provenance":"Tidligere Lorenz-dossier, fuldt indarbejdet 16.11.2026","imported_on":"2026-11-16"},{"title":"Earlier Woyten ancestry and collateral siblings","context":"Von Woyten–Kleist descent: supplied pedigree and source audit (16 November 2026)","text":"The supplied compilation adds Martin von Woyten, Anna von Puttkamer, Regina and Catharina von Woyten, and a proposed marriage of Regina to Gerson/Jerson von Kleist. The current 2025 Tychow-Dubberow entry for Christina does **not** establish these grandparents or siblings. A separate compiled *Ahnentafel Peters* and Geneanet profiles show a proposed Regina von Woyten–Gerson von Kleist pairing and descendants, but these are derivative genealogical compilations, not contemporary proof. Treat the proposed collateral line and the Puttkamer ancestry as **unverified leads**. In particular, the claim that Anna von Puttkamer was daughter of Lorentz/Peter/Martin von Puttkamer needs a precise source; the alternatives are not interchangeable identities.","provenance":"Tidligere Lorenz-dossier, fuldt indarbejdet 16.11.2026","imported_on":"2026-11-16"},{"title":"Next research actions","context":"Von Woyten–Kleist descent: supplied pedigree and source audit (16 November 2026)","text":"1. Obtain and inspect the full German 2025 edition at the exact references for II.55 (German edition p. 143; English translation p. 123), II.44 (German p. 36; English translation p. 29), II.60–84 and II.116–117. The English file states that it is machine-translated and still requires proofreading.\n2. Follow the footnote for the II.44 Christina claim to the cited von der Osten work; determine exactly what it says and whether it gives any source for the marriage.\n3. Search the original Pomeranian feudal, estate and church records cited in the family history (especially documents 571, 623 and 640 for II.55; and the records cited in the II.44 account). Seek evidence of Christina's widowhood, marriage sequence and the parentage of Hedwig Maria.\n4. Verify the II.82 family and Barbara Juliane–Cartz Ulrik marriage against the original marriage/estate documents and Danish church records; separately pursue Christian Adam's Vor Frelsers baptism image.\n5. Trace the later Danish line from Harbou (1897) and the relevant Danish church, probate and civil records before adding any further people or dates.\n\n**Assessment:** the Christina → Hedwig Maria → Christian/Carsten II.60 descent is supported as published genealogy. The identity of Christina with the Christina attributed to Georg II.44 remains unresolved. The Christian Casimir → Barbara Juliane → Cartz Ulrik → Christian Adam connection is supported by the 2025 family history and Harbou, but the primary records and later descendants still need independent verification. No new canonical people, dates or parent-child edges are added in this update.\n\nSources:\n- [2025 revised von Kleist family history, German edition](https://www.v-kleist.com/FG/Dubberow.pdf) — especially II.44 and II.55 (German pp. 36 and 143), II.60–84 and II.116–117.\n- [2025 English machine translation](https://v-kleist.com/FG/english/Dubberow_translation.pdf) — II.44 (English pp. 28–29), II.55 (p. 123), II.60–84 (pp. 139–143), II.81 and II.116–117 (pp. 141–149).\n- [Harbou, *Slægten von Kleist i Danmark* (1897)](https://www.v-kleist.com/FG_allg/Kleist_in_Daenemark.pdf).\n- [Ahnentafel Peters, vol. 1](https://dirkpeters.info/wp-content/uploads/2018/10/ahnentafel_peters_bd_1.pdf) — a compiled secondary pedigree; useful as a lead for Regina von Woyten and Gerson von Kleist, not as proof of the parentage.\n- Geneanet search lead for Christian Adam von Kleist (no unique individual record URL verified) — derivative tree data; use only as a clue; no clickable link until a unique record is identified.","provenance":"Tidligere Lorenz-dossier, fuldt indarbejdet 16.11.2026","imported_on":"2026-11-16"},{"title":"The Swenzone / Puttkamer ancestry: a significant correction","context":"Additional reverse pedigree: 19th-century Danish side branches and medieval claims (16 November 2026)","text":"The supplied account presents Anna von Puttkamer's family as reaching back to the Swenzone dynasty around 1257–1260. This is not a secure genealogical connection.\n\n- The Deutsche Biographie states that the Puttkamer name derives from the Polish office title *podkomorzy* (under-chamberlain), that the family is attested in the Stolp area in 1257, but that the proposed genealogical relationship to the Swenzones is unclear and modern scholarship is sceptical. It explicitly says the family is now considered probably immigrant rather than autochthonous Pomeranian, while its ethnic classification remains open.\n- Fritz Mörre's 1939 study, *Die Swenzonen in Ostpommern*, reviews the old claimed descent and concludes that there is no proof of a direct continuation from the Swenzones into the Puttkamer family. He explains that the 1425 \"Lorenz Swenz genannt Puttkamer\" was likely a member of the Swenz family, not a Puttkamer, and that Peter Putkummer appears as a vassal/witness in Swenzone documents rather than a proven relative.\n- Therefore, the supplied statement that the Puttkamer line is demonstrably descended from Swenzo should be removed from any factual narrative. It may be mentioned only as an older, disputed family tradition. The Puttkamer family itself is historically documented in Pomerania; that does not prove Anna von Puttkamer's exact parentage or connect her to Martin von Woyten.","provenance":"Tidligere Lorenz-dossier, fuldt indarbejdet 16.11.2026","imported_on":"2026-11-16"},{"title":"Stojentin and Woyten medieval origins","context":"Additional reverse pedigree: 19th-century Danish side branches and medieval claims (16 November 2026)","text":"- Valentin von Stojentin is independently documented as a Pomeranian councillor/court official, born around 1485 and dying in 1528 or 1529. This establishes a historical Stojentin figure, not that he was Christina's maternal grandfather or a direct ancestor of Elisabeth von Stojentin. The exact Giesebitz line still needs estate and church evidence.\n- Bagmihl's *Pommersches Wappenbuch* is a heraldic compilation. A coat of arms or a list of estates can help identify a family, but cannot by itself prove a parent-child relationship, the alleged Woyten etymology from *wojt/Wojciech*, or a descent from medieval Slavic nobility. The linguistic and ethnic claims in the supplied overview remain hypotheses pending specialist onomastic and archival sources.\n- The claims that Martin von Woyten held Varsin, Wessin, Alt Reitz and Vigelow, and that Benedictus von Woyten held Versin and served as a ducal captain/official at Bütow, remain useful leads. They still need exact references to contemporary feudal registers, land records or the relevant pages of a critical family history. Do not treat the proposed Martin → Benedictus father-son link or Anna von Puttkamer's parentage as established solely from the supplied pedigree.","provenance":"Tidligere Lorenz-dossier, fuldt indarbejdet 16.11.2026","imported_on":"2026-11-16"},{"title":"2. Earlier von Woyten ancestry — leads only","context":"2. Earlier von Woyten ancestry — leads only","text":"The supplied dossier proposes a paternal sequence:\n\n1. Michael von Woyten, described as an ensign in the service of Emperor Charles V.\n2. Conrad von Woyten.\n3. Conrad Pantaleon von Woyten.\n4. Vandemar von Woyten.\n5. Carl Woiten, said to have flourished around 1400.\n\nIt also names Martin von Woyten as a great-uncle of Benedictus.
